The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, said: "Hajj is the jihad of every weak person." He also said: "Whoever circumambulates this house seven times and prays two rak'ahs — the best prayer — will be forgiven." When he stood on the eve of Arafah, he faced the people and said: "Welcome to the delegation of Allah!" He said this three times, then said: "Those who ask shall be given, and those who fall short in this world shall be compensated. For every thousand dirhams spent, good deeds are written for them. O people, shall I not give you glad tidings? This evening, Allah Almighty commanded His angels, and they descended to the earth. Then He said to them: 'O My angels, look upon My servants — dusty and disheveled — who have come to Me from the ends of the earth. Do you know what they ask?'

<!-- page 137 -->
They will say, 'Our Lord, You know best,' and He will say, 'They ask Me for forgiveness, and I bear witness to you that I have forgiven them.'"

Hajj carries the reward of the Garden, and 'Umrah is expiation for every sin. He said, may peace be upon him and his family: "Alternate between Hajj and 'Umrah, for they negate poverty and sins and bring sustenance." He also said, peace be upon him: "There are sins that cannot be forgiven except at 'Arafat." And regarding the Hajj caravan, he said: "Do not turn them away" — meaning that their reward is written for them whether or not you conceal it. No veil shall be lifted from them unless their evil deeds are removed, and when they have fulfilled their rituals it will be said to them: "You have built a foundation, so do not demolish it. What has passed is enough for you, so do well in what is to come." The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said: "Whoever desires this world and the next — let him come to this House, for whatever a slave has asked of his Lord, Allah will grant him some of it or store it for him." Then he said: "O people, you must perform Hajj and 'Umrah and alternate between them, for they wash away sins as water washes away grime, and they negate poverty as fire removes the dross of iron."

Regarding him, may the prayers of Allah be upon him and his family, it is said that the righteous man who intends Hajj receives reward for all his deeds, meaning he is forgiven for what has preceded. Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, traced it back to the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, who said: "Pilgrims are of three kinds, and the best of them is the one who has been forgiven for his past and future sins. The second is the one who has been forgiven for his past sins and resumes good works. The third — and the least — is the one who has merely safeguarded his family and his wealth." The pilgrim must necessarily sustain some loss in his wealth, whether through the exertion itself or through the expense of maintenance. Whoever suffers loss in either his wealth or his family while on Hajj has lost one of the two rewards, but he is forgiven for what he has done and what he has delayed, his previous sins are forgiven, and he is called to fear Allah in what lies ahead.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: When Allah Almighty sent Adam, peace be upon him, down to the earth, he wandered to the House, just as he had seen the angels circling the Throne. He called out to his Lord asking for forgiveness,

<!-- page 138 -->
and the call came: "Allah has forgiven you." He said: "O Lord, and my descendants?" And the call came: "O Adam, whoever among your descendants comes to this place with their sin as you came with yours — Allah will forgive them." Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may the prayers of Allah be upon him, said: "The guarantee of the pilgrim who believes in Allah Almighty is that, even if he sins after returning to his family, he is under Allah's protection for seventy nights." He also said, may peace be upon him, regarding the words of Allah Almighty: "Purify My House for those who circumambulate it, those who remain in devotion, and those who bow and prostrate" [al-Baqarah: 125]: "When Allah Almighty revealed that, He sent down upon the Ka'bah one hundred and seventy mercies — sixty for those who circumambulate it, fifty for those who remain in devotion, forty for those who worship within it, and twenty for those who behold it."

A man asked Abu 'Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him: "Which is better — voluntary Hajj or charity?" He mentioned charity and its virtue. Abu 'Abdullah, may the prayers of Allah be upon him, kept insisting that Hajj is better, repeating it several times, until he looked toward the mountain of Abu Qubays and said: "Even if you were to give charity equal to its weight in gold and silver, you would not reach the reward of Hajj." Then he said: "Is there in charity a circumambulation of the House? Is there in charity a standing at 'Arafah?" The questioner said no. He said: "Hajj is better." May peace be upon him. He cited the words of Allah Almighty: "That they may witness benefits for them" [al-Hajj: 28], saying these are benefits for them in their religion. He said: "If a believing slave sets out from his home as a pilgrim, not a step does he take and not a stage of his journey passes except that good deeds are written for him, evil deeds are erased from him, and he is raised a degree. When he stands at 'Arafah, if he had sins equal to the number of the sands of the earth, they would be erased. When he throws the pebbles, his rank is raised until he completes the rite on the Day of Resurrection. When he shaves his head, for every hair that falls, Allah makes its place a light for him on the Day of Resurrection. Every expenditure he makes is written for him. And when he returns home, he is as the day his mother gave birth to him — sound from sins." Then he recited the words of Allah Almighty: "Whoever hastens his departure in two days — there is no sin on him, and whoever delays — there is no sin on him, for the one who fears Allah" [al-Baqarah: 203]. And he said, may peace be upon him:

<!-- page 139 -->
"No one stands upon those mountains — whether righteous or wicked — and calls upon Allah except that He responds to him. The righteous will be answered in the Hereafter, while others will receive their response in this world."

**On the Obligation of Hajj:**

Allah Almighty said: "And Hajj to the House is a duty owed to Allah by the people, for whoever is able to find a way to it. And whoever disbelieves — then indeed Allah is free from need of the worlds" [Al 'Imran: 97]. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said: When this verse was revealed, the people asked: "O Messenger of Allah, is it every year?" He remained silent. They asked him twice more, and he said: "No. If I had said yes, it would have become obligatory." Then Allah Almighty revealed: "O you who believe, do not ask about things that, if disclosed to you, would distress you" [al-Ma'idah: 101]. He also said, may the prayers of Allah be upon him, regarding the words of Allah Almighty — "And whoever disbelieves, then Allah is Independent of the worlds" [Al 'Imran: 97]: "This is said of the one who abandons Hajj despite being able to perform it."

Ja'far ibn Muhammad was asked, may the prayers of Allah be upon him, about the saying of Allah Almighty: "And Hajj to the House is a duty owed to Allah by the people, for whoever is able to find a way to it" [Al 'Imran: 97] — what constitutes "a way"? The questioner relayed what people say about it: that it means provisions and a mount. Abu 'Abdullah, may the prayers of Allah be upon him, said: "Abu Ja'far was asked about this, may peace be upon him, and he said: 'The people are ruined by such a view. Even if a man has nothing but provisions, he must set out and not say to his children, "What shall we do?" — they are ruined.' When asked what he can say, the answer was: 'The ability to travel, sufficient provision for the journey, the presence of what sustains those left behind, security of the road — and note that Allah Almighty did not make zakat obligatory except upon those who possess two hundred dirhams.'"

He was asked, may peace be upon him, about the man who wishes to perform Hajj but is prevented only by a trade that occupies him or

<!-- page 140 -->
a debt he owes. He said: "There is no excuse for him — he must perform Hajj, and if he dies having left it, he has abandoned one of the laws of Islam." He said, may peace be upon him: "Whoever dies without having performed Hajj — not prevented by fear, nor by an illness that prevented him from bearing the journey, nor by a ruler who blocked him — then he dies as a Jew or a Christian." He also said: "Hajj is obligatory upon a person once in his lifetime. Whoever abandons it while able, until he dies, is among those of whom Allah Almighty said: 'And We will gather him on the Day of Resurrection blind' — and when it is said to him, 'But you had sight,' he will say, 'Indeed, but I was blind to the path of the truth and the good.'" The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said: "If my ummah were to abandon this House and neglect it, they would not be left without punishment."

He was asked about a child performing Hajj, and he said: "It does not count for him, and he is obliged to perform Hajj when he reaches the age of majority — if he has come to know this matter and Allah has favored him with that knowledge. His Hajj will be rewarded out of consideration for him." He also said: "Hajj is more beloved to me than the Hajj of a Nasib who believes the Resurrection will occur — let him perform Hajj while upon that belief, and then when Allah grants him knowledge, he must perform Hajj again if he is able." He said, may peace be upon him, regarding the enslaved person: "He is rewarded for Hajj as long as he is owned. If he is freed, he is then obliged to perform Hajj if he is able — there is no obligation upon him while he is owned." Likewise, the mother of a child held by her master: she receives no reward for it. A man may not prevent his wife from performing an obligatory Hajj if she finds one who is a mahram to accompany her, who can travel with her and provide for her, and the road is secure. He also said, may peace be upon him: "A divorced woman may perform Hajj during her 'iddah if she wishes." If a man is insolvent, another man may perform Hajj on his behalf. He was asked about this, may peace be upon him, and said: "It is his duty as it stands, just as Hajj is obligatory." "And complete the Hajj and 'Umrah for Allah" [al-Baqarah: 196] — He said: complete them. He said: he may choose whichever he wishes — Hajj or 'Umrah. Whoever wishes to enter ihram from the miqat may do so from the miqat. It is not permissible for the muhrim to shave his head until he has fulfilled the rite, because Allah Almighty says: "Do not shave your heads until the offering reaches its place"


<!-- page 141 -->
Guidance is established in its proper place. Whoever does not have a sacrificial animal — that is, a horn — may enjoy the relief of ihram upon arriving in Mecca, performing tawaf and sa'y. Then it becomes obligatory to enter ihram for Hajj from Mecca. Allah Almighty said: "Whoever enjoys Umrah until Hajj" — meaning, upon arriving in Mecca. Abu 'Abd Allah said: He is entitled to a sheep or more.

- Mentioning what one who wishes to perform Hajj should do:

Allah Almighty said: "Hajj is during well-known months, so whoever has made Hajj obligatory upon himself therein, let there be no lewdness nor wickedness nor quarreling in Hajj." [al-Baqarah: 197] Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Abd Allah (peace be upon him) said: The months of Hajj are Shawwal, Dhu al-Qi'dah, and Dhu al-Hijjah; Hajj is not obligatory in any other months. The obligation of Hajj entails meeting its requirements, fulfilling its prescribed acts, and following its model. For one who intends Hajj, refusal of lewdness becomes obligatory: this means refraining from intercourse, indecency, lying, cursing, and quarreling — saying "no, by Allah" and "yes, by Allah" — as well as boasting.

'Ali ibn al-Husayn (peace be upon him) said to Abu Khalid al-Wasiti, in advising him when he wished to perform Hajj: "O Abu Khalid, if you go to Mecca — God willing — then fear Allah, remember Him abundantly, and speak little except in good, for the completion of Hajj and Umrah lies in a man guarding his tongue. As Allah said: 'No lewdness, no wickedness, no quarreling in Hajj.' Lewdness means intercourse; wickedness means lying, cursing, and quarreling; and quarreling is a man's saying 'no, I swear' and 'yes, I swear' and bragging. You must be pious, restraining yourself from sins against Allah; be forbearing, so as to master your anger; and keep good company with those who travel with you. There is no power except by Allah."

Abu 'Abd Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said to a man he was advising: "If you go out on pilgrimage, you must fear Allah, be forbearing, be pious, and keep good company with those who accompany you, for you are" — and Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (peace be upon him) said: "He does not care about whoever leads the journey to this House if he does not possess

<!-- page 142 -->
three qualities: piety that keeps him away from sins against Allah, forbearance with which he masters his anger, and good companionship for those who travel with him."

Abu 'Abd Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said to a man who wished to perform Hajj, recommending him: "When you depart from your home intending Hajj or Umrah, recite the relief prayer and say: 'There is no god but Allah, the Forbearing, the Generous; there is no god but Allah, the Most High, the Great; glory be to Allah, Lord of the seven heavens and Lord of the seven earths and Lord of the Throne; and praise be to Allah, Lord of the worlds. O Allah, open before me goodness, and make me well; and protect me from every evil creature whose forelock You hold — for You are my Lord upon a straight path. In the name of Allah I have set out, and in the name of Allah I enter, and for the sake of Allah I walk. O Allah, I present to You in my hands my forgetfulness and my shortcomings. In the name of Allah, and by the will of Allah, in this journey of mine — whatever I remember of it or have forgotten — O Allah, You are the Helper over all affairs, and You are the Companion in travel and the Successor over family, wealth, and children. May Allah make our journey easy for us and cover the earth for us and let us walk upon it in obedience to You and obedience to Your Messenger. O Allah, set right our affairs and bless us in what You have provided us, and save us from the torment of the Fire.'"

When you set your leg among the travelers, say: "In the name of Allah, and by Allah, and Allah is the Greatest." When you are settled in your journey, praise Allah seven times, glorify Him seven times, magnify Him seven times, and say "La ilaha ill Allah" seven times, and say: "Praise be to Allah who guided us to Islam and honored us with Muhammad, peace be upon him and his family." Glory be to the One who has subjected this to us, for we could not have subdued it ourselves, and indeed to our Lord we shall return. Praise be to Allah, Lord of the worlds. O Allah, You are the Carrier upon the back and the Helper in affairs. O Allah, grant us the best of news, and lead us to Your forgiveness and Your pleasure. O Allah, no bird flies but by Your leave, and no good comes but from You, and none else protects. O Allah, I seek refuge in You from the hardships of travel, from the distress of turning back, and from an ill return to family, wealth,

<!-- page 143 -->
and children. O Allah, I ask You in this journey of mine for piety, for contentment, and for deeds that please You. O Allah, cut short the distance for me and make it easy, and set aright my affairs and leave me well among my family." When you return, say in your return: "We are those who repent, who worship, who praise — returning to our Lord, giving thanks, devoted, and to Him we return."

- Mentioning the entry into the city of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him):

The Commander of the Believers (peace be upon him) addressed in his sermon, saying: The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) declared Madinah sacred between the mountains of 'Ayr and Thawr. Whoever causes an unlawful act therein or shelters one who commits it — the curse of Allah, the angels, and all people is upon him, and Allah will not accept from him any obligatory or supererogatory act.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said: The two sides of Madinah are sanctified. When asked what was not sanctified, he said: "Whatever the free woman surrounded with her garment — that is, what the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) prohibited: disturbing its inhabitants, hunting its game, and cutting its trees."

It is recommended to perform a ritual bath before entering the city, and to begin the visit at the Mosque of the Prophet (peace be upon him and his family). One should stand at his grave, offer prayers upon him and his family, and testify to his prophethood and message. One should pray whatever one is able, and then pray two rak'ahs before sitting at the pillar that is advanced on the side of the grave from the corner of the grave — that is, the place nearest the head of the Prophet (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him). One should then rise, approach the grave, and say: "I bear witness that there is no god but Allah alone, with no partner; and I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and messenger; and I testify that you are the Messenger of Allah, truly; and I testify that you are Muhammad ibn 'Abd Allah; and I testify that you conveyed the message of your Lord and gave sincere counsel

<!-- page 144 -->
to your nation, and that you strove in the way of Allah, and that you worshipped Allah until certainty came to you, and you fulfilled what was due upon you. This is the clear truth. May Allah reward you with the best of rewards. O Allah, bless Your servant and messenger Muhammad — as You have described him, as befits his faithfulness and the best of Your creation — and upon the family of Muhammad, the best, most complete, and most beautiful blessings as You prayed upon one of Your prophets, Your purified ones, and the people of dignity, for You are Glorious and Generous."

"O Allah, bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad as You blessed Noah in the worlds; and bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad as You blessed Moses and Aaron; and bless Muhammad and the family of Muhammad as You blessed Ibrahim and the family of Ibrahim — for You are the Praised, the Glorious. O Allah, I have nothing with which to begin asking You, whether I have asked or not; whether I pronounced it or not; whether I knew it from myself or did not — I ask You by the truth of Your messenger Muhammad and his blessed and noble grave, to graciously attend to all my needs, small and large."

He (peace be upon him) said: "If you are able to remain in the city for three days — Wednesday, Thursday, and one day of Friday — then pray on Wednesday between the grave and the pulpit at the pillar that adjoins the grave, and strive and ask Allah your needs for this world and the hereafter. Pray likewise on Thursday at the pillar of repentance. Pray on Friday at the shrine of the Prophet (peace be upon him and his family), opposite the large pillar, and fast those three days."

The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings of Allah be upon him) said: "Whoever visits my grave after my death is as one who migrated to me during my life. And whoever cannot visit my grave, let him convey peace upon me — it will be reported to me." Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said: It is desirable that

<!-- page 145 -->
whoever witnesses the city should also visit the Mosque of Quba' — which is the mosque founded on piety — and al-Fadikh Mosque, al-Fath Mosque, and the Mosque of Umm Ibrahim; and pray there, and visit the grave of Hamza (peace be upon him) and the graves of the martyrs, may Allah have mercy on them. The last act of the pilgrim in Madinah should be to visit the grave of the Prophet (peace be upon him and his family) and bid him farewell, saying to him what was said before.

- Mentioning the miqats:

The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) designated the miqat sites for entering ihram at five places for the people of Madinah and those allied with them: al-Shajarah Mosque; for the people of Syria and those beyond it, al-Juhfah; for the people of Yemen, Yalamlam; for the people of al-Ta'if, Qarn; and for the people of Najd, al-'Aqiq.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said: These are the miqats for ihram for those who wish to perform Hajj or Umrah, or both together. To complete Hajj and Umrah, ihram must be assumed from the miqats that the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) designated. One must not pass them without entering ihram, and one does not enter ihram before reaching them. Whoever enters ihram before the miqat has violated the Sunnah; if he omits something from his ihram, nothing is required of him because he was not truly in ihram. He does not enter ihram until he does so from the miqat. He said: "Whoever's home is closer to Mecca than the miqats, let him enter ihram from his home; he is not required to travel to the miqat."

He (peace be upon him) also said, as part of the completion of Hajj and Umrah: "Enter ihram in the vicinity of your family." He (peace be upon him) also said: "Whoever fears that the month of Umrah will pass — let him enter ihram before the miqat. If he departs in Rajab intending Umrah but knows he will not reach the miqat until Sha'ban, he must not leave ihram until he reaches the miqat; thus his Umrah becomes

<!-- page 146 -->
a Sha'bani Umrah. But if he enters ihram in the remaining days of Rajab before the miqat, his Umrah is a Rajabi Umrah, for Rajabiyyah is better — and that was his intention."

He also said regarding one who passed beyond the tree and did not enter ihram: "What lies between him and al-Juhfah, let him enter ihram from there." He said: "Whoever comes to the miqat and forgets to enter ihram, or was ignorant of it, until he passed it — or who knowingly proceeded to Makkah — he is in a state requiring rectification. If he was able to return to the miqat, he should return and enter ihram from it. If he feared missing Hajj and could not return, he should enter ihram from his present location. If he was in Mecca, he should go outside the Haram and enter ihram there; if he enters the Haram in a state of ihram, let him proceed accordingly. Otherwise, he should enter ihram from his place."

- Remembrance of the Ihram:

The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family) commanded those who were with him during the Farewell Pilgrimage, when they reached al-Shajarah, to pluck the armpits, shave the pubic hair, and remove sewn garments and robes.

It is narrated from Abu 'Abd Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) that he said: "Trim your nails, take from your moustache, and do not harm yourself in any way." He began with a written or supererogatory prayer — preferable after the sun has passed its zenith, though it is not restricted to any time; ihram may be assumed by day or night at any time when prayer is permissible. He said: The menstruating woman, upon arriving at the miqat, should perform a ritual bath and enter ihram. Likewise for the women who said that the muhrim should not perfume himself before ihram in such a way that the scent remains on him after ihram. If he enters ihram and does not reach the miqat, nothing is upon him; his ihram proceeds. He followed the prayer and said (peace be upon him): "If the muhrim wishes to enter ihram, he makes his intention for it and speaks after the prayer, saying what ihram is forbidden for him — such as Hajj, or Umrah, or Hajj alone, or Umrah alone. Or he says: 'I intend to enjoy Umrah unto Hajj.' Or he says: 'O Allah, I intend to combine Hajj with Umrah'" — if he had a sacrificial animal with him — "or he says: 'O Allah, I intend Hajj' or 'Umrah,' whichever he wishes. And let him mention it upon Your Book

and the Sunnah of Your Prophet. O Allah, and my place is wherever You have detained me by Your decree — for You are capable over me. O Allah, I mean" — that which shall make it accepted from him. Then he prays with whatever supplications he wishes and intends what he wishes to do — this is established without articulating it in all its parts. He (peace be upon him) said: "The best Hajj is tamattu' — Umrah unto Hajj — which is the form upon which the Qur'an was revealed." The Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him and his family), when he performed the Farewell Hajj, had combined Hajj and Umrah because he had brought the sacrificial animal. When he finished in Makkah, he circumambulated the House and performed sa'y between Safa and Marwah, and Allah Almighty revealed to him what He revealed. He said: "Had I received this command before, I would not have brought the sacrificial animal and would have made it a tamattu'. So whoever does not


<!-- page 147 -->
He should have a guide with him. He will then release the sacrificial animal of the people and make it an Umrah, except for those who had a sacrificial animal with them. It is then forbidden to perform Hajj from the Sacred Mosque on the Day of Tarwiyah, so enjoying Umrah until Hajj is what he commanded. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) said this to those who were not present at the Grand Mosque. As for those who were from the people of the Haram, they do not have the right to enjoy Umrah to Hajj, because they are able to perform Umrah whenever they like. But Allah Almighty has expanded that for those who came from other lands, allowing them to perform Umrah and Hajj in a single trip as an extension of His mercy and a concession for them.

Whoever enjoys Umrah to Hajj, or singles out Umrah and enters ihram, then comes to Makkah and performs tawaf of the House seven circuits and prays two rak'ahs of tawaf, then walks between al-Safa and al-Marwah seven circuits — beginning with al-Safa and ending with al-Marwah only — he has completed his Umrah. He should then release himself from ihram if he intends Tamattu', and trim from the ends of his hair and nails. He remains in a state resembling that of one in ihram until the Day of Tarwiyah, when he enters ihram for Hajj from the Sacred Mosque, just as he would enter ihram from the miqat. If he has a sacrificial animal, it is not released and he remains in his ihram until he completes the Hajj, as Allah Almighty says: "And do not shave your heads until the sacrificial animal reaches its place of slaughter" [al-Baqarah: 196]. As for whoever singles out Hajj and comes to Makkah, he need not perform tawaf until he returns from Arafah; if he performs voluntary tawaf, that is good and praiseworthy. And if he comes from Mina without entering Makkah, there is no harm in that.

---

**Mentioning imitation, poetry, glorification, and talbiyyah:**

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali (peace be upon him) said: In former times, people used to mark sacrificial camels, cows, and sheep for offering. Then the tradition of marking cows and sheep was abandoned. He said: to mark an animal means to tie a belt or a string — something sewn — around it, and this is done for animals designated for Hajj and Umrah after entering ihram at the miqat, as a prescribed act of devotion.

<!-- page 148 -->
The right side of the animal's hump is cut with an iron instrument until blood flows, so that it is known to be a sacrificial animal. It is recommended that a sandal be hung around its neck — one in which its owner has prayed — so that if the animal strays, it is recognized as a sacrificial offering. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said: you anoint the body of the animal on the right side of its hump, and it is glorified and led along. When one reaches al-Bayda' — the point at which those who have not yet entered ihram do so, where people gather and the new moon is sighted — the pilgrim raises his voice and says: "Labbayk, Allahumma labbayk, labbayk la sharika laka labbayk, inna al-hamda wa al-ni'mata laka wa al-mulk, la sharika lak." He likewise repeats this talbiyyah whenever he ascends a height or descends into a valley, when he encounters other travelers, on the plain of every obligatory prayer, upon waking from sleep, and at the break of dawn — whether in a state of ritual purity or not, though purity is preferable.

It is recommended for a man to raise his voice with the talbiyyah, while women should lower their voices to the extent that they themselves can hear. Whoever needs to ride the sacrificial animal out of necessity may do so and drink its milk when needed; this is not harmful if there is a compelling reason. If the animal is one vowed or offered as expiation, its rider is liable to replace it; if it is a voluntary offering, there is nothing upon him. Whatever is guaranteed must not be eaten from if slaughtered — it must be given entirely in charity. As for what was a voluntary offering, its owner may eat from it, feed others, and give in charity.

---

**Mentioning what is forbidden to the one in ihram and what he must do if this occurs:**

Allah Almighty said: "Hajj is in well-known months, and whoever has obligated Hajj upon himself therein — then there is no sexual relations and no disobedience and no disputing during Hajj" [al-Baqarah: 197]. It has been said that "no debauchery" refers to intercourse. He said: "And do not kill game while you are in the state of ihram" [al-Ma'idah: 95], and He said: "Forbidden to you is game from the land as long as you are in the state of ihram" [al-Ma'idah: 96].

From the Ahl al-Bayt (peace be upon them): one in ihram is forbidden from hunting, intercourse, sexual acts,

<!-- page 149 -->
wearing sewn clothing, cutting hair, trimming nails, and other things that we will mention in this chapter and in the chapter that follows it, along with what is obligatory for each violation, insha'Allah. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said: If a man deliberately has intercourse with his wife while in ihram, knowing that this is not permissible for him, he has incurred a penalty upon himself. If he does not complete the circuits of the House on the Day of Sacrifice, he must complete the Hajj and then make it up the following year, even if his wife is a woman who knew that this was not permissible for her — she too incurs a penalty. If he was unaware and she was asleep, there is nothing upon her, but there are two penalties upon him. If both are ignorant that this is not permissible for them, there is nothing upon the one who lacks knowledge, while the knowledgeable one must expiate; and the Hajj of whoever was intimate in ignorance is still valid. If the intimacy occurred before the first relief, the penalty applies and he is not required to make up the Hajj if the intimacy came afterward. If he forgot, there is nothing upon him, and he is like one who ate and drank during the daytime of Ramadan in forgetfulness.

It is not permissible for one in ihram to contract a marriage during his ihram, nor to conduct the marriage of another. If he does so, the marriage is void, whether he knows that this is not permissible or claims to know the woman was never lawful for him. If he was ignorant, they are separated; he may marry her thereafter if he wishes. If the muhrim begins intercourse with his wife, or kisses her with desire, or deliberately looks at her with lust, or touches her with desire, a blood penalty of one sheep is incurred. But if it was unintentional and without desire, it is only disliked and there is nothing upon him.

If the muhrim swears an oath — saying "No, by Allah" or "Yes, by Allah" — and he is truthful, there is nothing upon him even if he repeats it three or more times. But if it were a false oath, one blood penalty is upon him for a single instance, and it increases with repetition. If the muhrim shaves his head, one sheep is upon him. If he removes some hair from his head or beard, charity equivalent to that amount is due. If he merely wipes his head or beard and something falls out, there is nothing upon him. He said: If the muhrim needs cupping and shaves the area, charity is due. If he clips all his nails, blood is due; if he clips some of them, charity proportional to what was clipped is due. It is permissible for the muhrim, if he cannot find sandals,

<!-- page 150 -->
to wear slippers that do not extend above the heels. He said: If the muhrim fears being left completely unclothed, he may remain in his clothing while in ihram, and the recompense for that is the blood of a sheep, or charity, or fasting in proportion. It is permissible for the muhrim to have himself circumcised if needed. He may use kohl if he needs it, and there is no harm in that; it is even praiseworthy if he intends treatment. He should not use it for adornment unless it is medicinal. It is disliked for the muhrim to be bled unless there is a reason; it is permissible if he descends and removes ihram clothing or does something forbidden while in ihram out of ignorance or forgetfulness — there is no harm upon him in such a case.

He said: There is no harm in entering ihram in a dyed garment unless it is dyed with saffron, wars, or perfume. White is better, and it is the garment that is commanded; if unavailable, other garments may be used. He said: It is permissible for a woman in ihram to wear ornaments unless she displays them to unrelated men. It is permissible for the muhrim to remove whatever causes him harm. He said: The male muhrim must not cover his head, nor the female muhrim cover her face; however, a garment may fall upon the male muhrim's head without him covering his ears. It is permissible for him to secure a bag upon himself. If he covers his head, or a woman in ihram covers her face, charity is due from each of them. It is permissible to wash while in ihram, though submerging the head in water is disliked.

---

**Mentioning the recompense for hunting that befalls the one in ihram:**

Allah Almighty said: "O you who believe, do not kill game while you are in the state of ihram. And whoever among you kills it intentionally, his recompense is the equivalent of what he killed, judged by two just men among you" [al-Ma'idah: 95]. This is how the Ahl al-Bayt (peace be upon them) recited it — "judged by one who is just," meaning the Imam (peace be upon him). There is a lengthy argument for this which we have mentioned elsewhere, abridged here.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him) said, regarding Allah's words: "And whoever repeats it, Allah will take retribution from him" [al-Ma'idah: 95]: whoever kills game while in ihram is judged

<!-- page 151 -->
to pay its equivalent. If he kills another animal thereafter, he is not judged again — rather, Allah takes retribution from him — but the expiation is only for the first instance. He said: Whoever kills game while in ihram must pay its equivalent in livestock. If he was performing Hajj, he slaughters the sacrificial animal as a gift in Mina; if he was performing Umrah, he slaughters it in Makkah. If he cannot find such an animal, he gives charity equivalent to its price. If he cannot give charity, he is obligated to feed sixty poor people; if he cannot do that, he fasts for eighteen days.

Whoever is obligated to offer a cow and cannot find one feeds thirty poor people; if he cannot, he fasts nine days. Whoever is obligated to offer a sheep and cannot find one gives charity to ten poor people; if he cannot, he fasts three days. He said: Whoever cannot find anything — neither a sacrificial animal, nor the means to give charity — begins fasting; if he finds the means afterward, he continues fasting as he wills, and his recompense is fulfilled.

He said (peace be upon him): If a person in ihram strikes an ostrich or a zebra, he owes one camel for each. For a wild cow, he owes a cow. For a gazelle, a dove, or similar birds, he owes one sheep for each. If he breaks the eggs of an ostrich, the camel stallion is placed over a number of she-camels corresponding to the number of eggs. Whatever is born from them is given as a sacrificial offering; if nothing is born, there is nothing upon him, because the eggs are like that. If he breaks an egg and finds chicks inside it, he must place the camel stallion over she-camels until they are inseminated and their offspring stir in their wombs; whatever emerges thereafter is given as a gift, and whatever is miscarried or dies entails nothing upon him, because the chicks — like those in eggs — may die before hatching. If he strikes chicks that have developed souls within them, he places the camel stallion over a number of she-camels until they are inseminated and their offspring stir in their wombs; whatever is born from them is given as a gift, and whatever is miscarried or dies entails nothing upon him, because the chicks in the eggs, after souls have entered them, may die. This is the basis regarding eggs struck by a person in ihram, and it has only been known through the Imams of the Ahl al-Bayt (peace be upon them).

It is narrated that an Arab man came to 'Umar (may Allah be pleased with him) during his time and said: O Commander of the Faithful, I set out for Hajj while in ihram and passed by a resting place — that is, a place

<!-- page 152 -->
where an ostrich had gathered its eggs and fortified them. I broke the eggs and grilled and ate them. What do you see for me? He said: Go to your companions and they will judge you, and I will confirm their judgment for you. So he went to some of the Companions of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family) who were sitting in the mosque. He asked them and they disagreed among themselves. He returned to 'Umar and said: Here is a man whom we disagreed about — you should rule in his matter. So 'Umar went to him and asked, and said: Who is he? He was told: 'Ali ibn Abi Talib. He went to him while al-Hasan and al-Husayn (peace be upon them) were with him, and told him the story.

He asked him for the ruling, and 'Ali said to him: Ask these two. He went to al-Hasan and al-Husayn (peace be upon them). The Arab said: What have I found from you, O family of Muhammad? Whenever I asked one of you, he referred me to another, until you sent me to the boys. He rose to leave, and some of those present said: These are the sons of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family), so ask them, O Arab — you will find what you seek. He asked them, and they said: You must place a camel stallion over a number of she-camels corresponding to the broken eggs; whatever is born from them is given as a sacrificial offering. The Arab said to 'Ali (peace be upon him): Is that so? He said: Yes, O Arab. The man left. 'Umar saw him and sent for him, asking about what had occurred, and the man told him. 'Umar said: Go back and tell him that the she-camels may miscarry — that is, the fetuses may be lost before birth. He went back and reported this, and 'Ali said to him: Tell him also that the eggs may be addled — that is, spoiled so that no chick develops from them. He went and told 'Umar, who said: This is why we have been commanded to ask them.

Another narration states that 'Umar asked him about it himself. The explanation regarding the chicks that sustain injury is that their living and dying are taken into account. This was narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon them), on the basis transmitted from him (peace be upon him): he said that if a person in ihram strikes a wild cow, a cow is due from him. If he cannot find one, he feeds thirty poor people; if he cannot do that, he fasts for nine days. They said:


<!-- page 153 -->
In the case of the fawn, a person in a state of iḥrām who strikes it owes a sheep. If he cannot provide a sheep, he must feed ten poor people. If he cannot do that, he fasts three days. The same applies to anyone upon whom a sheep is obligatory as a hunting penalty, and we shall mention what is required in that regard.

Whoever is obliged to pay a penalty but cannot find the equivalent animal, cannot feed the poor, and is unable to fast, then nothing further is required of him. Allah the Almighty has said: "Allah does not burden a soul except to its capacity" [Al-Baqarah: 286], and: "Allah does not burden a soul except with what He has given it" [Al-Ṭalāq: 7]. Such a person has no remaining obligation, as he is incapable of fulfilling it.

The one obligated by a penalty is bound by what he has been obliged to make up, and when he becomes able to fast, he fasts — in accordance with what we have mentioned. They said, peace be upon them, that if a person in a state of iḥrām kills a hyena, a fox, or a rabbit during the sacred months, a sheep is owed for each one, or its equivalent in feeding the poor or fasting if the sheep cannot be provided, as we have described.

They also said, peace be upon them, that the prescribed penalty is meant as a deterrent against hunting. Furthermore, they held that a person in iḥrām who kills a pigeon, or a bird resembling it such as the wood pigeon, the turtle dove, the partridge, or any similar bird, owes a sheep for each, or its equivalent in feeding or fasting if a sheep is unavailable. If eggs are damaged, a portion of the sheep's value is owed, in proportion to the ruling established for one who damages ostrich eggs — for which a camel is prescribed — as has been mentioned previously.

They said, peace be upon them, that if a person in iḥrām during the sacred months kills a monitor lizard (ḍabb), a jerboa (yarbū'), or anything similar, he owes a penalty for each one. They also said, peace be upon them, that if someone in iḥrām kills a game animal while in the non-sacred territory (ḥalāl), the same penalty as described above applies. If the animal is killed within the sacred precinct (ḥaram), both the penalty and the animal's monetary value are owed in charity, in order to make the prohibition more severe, to honour the sacred precinct, and to ensure that nothing Allah the Almighty and His Messenger have forbidden is treated as inconsequential.

<!-- page 154 -->
If a single game animal is killed by a group of people in a state of iḥrām, each one of them individually bears the full penalty, because each has committed the transgression and violated the prohibition. They are forbidden to hunt anything else, and this ruling does not conflict with or undermine other rulings. They permitted the person in iḥrām who is obligated to pay a penalty to eat of the catch and then pay the penalty, but he may not eat of an animal found dead when a slaughtered animal is available.

They said, peace be upon them, that if a person in iḥrām throws something at a game animal and wounds it, or breaks its leg or hand, and the animal then flees and he does not know its fate, he owes the full penalty. If he catches the animal and it is sound, and then he releases it, he owes a quarter of the penalty for breaking its leg or hand, and the proportional blood money or equivalent penalty is owed in charity for wounding it.

If the person in iḥrām slaughters the game animal as his penalty, he may not eat it, nor may anyone else feed from it whether in the ḥalāl territory or within the ḥaram. If he does so, he must pay the penalty again. They instructed that the animal be buried so that it would not be eaten. If it is exhumed after burial, there is nothing further upon the person, provided he did not order the exhumation, and there is no evidence requiring anything of him in that case. This applies only to the one who killed the animal.

They said, peace be upon them, that if a person in iḥrām kills a game animal by mistake or in ignorance, he still owes the full penalty, just as if he had done so deliberately. This is the position agreed upon by many scholars. Allah the Almighty says: "O you who believe, do not kill game while you are in iḥrām. Whoever among you kills it deliberately shall be penalised with the equivalent of what he killed from livestock" [Al-Māʾidah: 95]. The Imams, peace be upon them, know best the interpretation of Allah's Book. Nothing in this verse, nor anything on the tongue of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, explicitly removes the penalty from the person in iḥrām who kills game by mistake.

As for the narration that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, said that Allah has lifted from his nation the consequences of mistakes and forgetfulness — this does not apply here. It means that the person is relieved of sin when he did not act deliberately. However, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and his family, made blood money obligatory for accidental killing,

<!-- page 155 -->
along with what is obligatory for wounds inflicted upon a sane person — and this does not invalidate that principle. The penalty is not waived for the person in iḥrām who accidentally kills game, as there is nothing in either the Book or the Sunnah to remove it from the one who committed it.

The basis for this is found in the words of Allah the Almighty: "O you who believe, Allah will test you with something of the game that your hands and your spears reach" [Al-Māʾidah: 94]. The verse addresses those in iḥrām, and whatever a person in iḥrām takes — whether by his hand or his weapon — falls within the matters Allah the Almighty has initiated, even though what is established from the Imams, peace be upon them, is not in itself a proof obligating those who follow their rulings — unless it is because Allah the Almighty has linked obedience to them with obedience to Him and to His Messenger, may Allah bless him and his family. What is established from the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, is a proof for those who accept it, as is the consensus of the Ummah, since they are also obliged to follow what has been reported from the Imams, may Allah's prayers be upon them. The full argument on this point is lengthy and has been mentioned elsewhere; what we have cited here is sufficient for one who agrees to accept the truth, God willing.

They said, peace be upon them, that if a person in iḥrām catches a fish in the sacred precinct (ḥaram), he must release it. Likewise, if someone buys a fish that was caught in the ḥaram while he is in iḥrām, he must return it.

If a person in iḥrām kills small birds such as sparrows, swifts, or similar creatures, a handful of food is owed for each one. For locusts and similar creatures, and for wasps, bees, and the like, a handful of food is owed if they are deliberately killed. If one of them alights on a person or he inadvertently sits on it and it dies, there is nothing upon him. The same applies to insects of the earth such as beetles and the like, as well as ants, corn weevils, flies, mosquitoes, ticks, and lice.

They permitted the person in iḥrām to kill whatever harms or endangers him, such as harmful flies, scorpions, snakes, birds of prey, the four types of vermin, dogs, and scorpions.

<!-- page 156 -->
All of these may be killed freely, and there is nothing upon the person in iḥrām for killing them. They permitted him to scare off crows and magpies, and they allowed the person in iḥrām to engage in sea fishing, as Allah the Almighty has explicitly permitted it in His Book, making it lawful for him to catch, eat, and provision from sea fish.

They said, peace be upon them, regarding water birds and wading birds: whatever of them lays its eggs on land is considered a land creature, and whatever lays its eggs in water is considered sea catch. They also said that birds are only considered game when caught in flight. As for domesticated chickens and birds kept in homes and urban areas — such as Sindhi chickens and the like — these are not a matter of hunting.

They said, peace be upon them, that whoever is obligated to pay a penalty for hunting must slaughter it in Minā if he is a ḥajj pilgrim, or in Makkah if he is performing ʿUmrah.

---

**On the Ḥaram: Its Sanctity, Entry, and What Is Permissible Within It**

Allah the Almighty said: "Have they not seen that We have made a safe sanctuary?" [Al-ʿAnkabūt: 67]. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, forbade hunting within the ḥaram and cutting down its trees.

It is reported that on the day of the conquest of Makkah, the Prophet, may Allah's prayers be upon him, stood with his back to the Kaʿbah, holding the door that had been opened for him, while the people of Makkah and others gathered before him. He proclaimed: "There is no god but Allah, alone, with no partner. He fulfilled His promise, granted victory to His servant, and defeated the confederates alone." He then said: "What do you say, O people of Makkah, and what do you expect?" They replied: "We say good, and we think well of a generous brother and a generous nephew who has power." He said: "I say as my brother Yūsuf said: 'No blame upon you today; may Allah forgive you, and He is the most merciful of the merciful.' But Allah the Almighty made Makkah sacred on the day He created the heavens and the earth, and it remains forbidden until the Day of Judgement. Its game shall not be disturbed, its trees shall not be cut, and the lost property found in it shall not be taken except to announce it." Al-ʿAbbās said: "O Messenger of Allah, with the exception of idhkhir, for it is used in graves and houses."

<!-- page 157 -->
Al-ʿAbbās meant that the people used to place idhkhir over the dead in their graves to protect them from dust, to lay it beneath them, to use it on the roofs of their houses, and that goldsmiths burned it as fuel. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, then said: "Except idhkhir." These words were reported in several versions narrated from the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, and this is what defines and preserves the sanctity of the ḥaram.

He, may Allah's prayers be upon him and his family, commanded that no trees of the ḥaram be struck down and that its game not be hunted. He made it permissible to take from it only what is necessary, such as a stick for a shepherd or what is genuinely needed, along with fruits and provisions, and what people require for their homes. He did not prohibit animals from grazing and feeding from it, nor from what grows naturally in the ḥaram without human cultivation. Whoever damages anything of the ḥaram should give charity according to its value.

It is recommended that one perform a ritual bath (ghusl) before entering the ḥaram. The person performing a combined ḥajj and ʿumrah (mutamatti') should stop reciting the talbiyah when he catches sight of the houses of Makkah, then raise his voice in praise. The person performing ḥajj stops the talbiyah when the sun sets on the Day of ʿArafah. The person performing ʿumrah alone stops it when he enters the ḥaram.

When the ḥajj pilgrim, ʿumrah performer, ḥajj-and-ʿumrah combiner (qārin), or mutamattī' enters Makkah, he should begin by attending to his travel needs and storing his belongings — if he has a companion and belongings — before performing any rites. After that, he should go to the Sacred Mosque. He should approach it with reverence and dignity, believing that the House is to be glorified. It is better if he walks to it barefoot, though riding or travelling by other means is also permissible. It is recommended that he enter through Bāb Banī Shayba, which is known as the Gate of the Iraqis. He should recite whatever prayers are within his ability and ease at that time.

Jaʿfar ibn Muḥammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, instructed as follows: when you have entered Makkah and put down your travel gear, begin with the Sacred Mosque. When you approach it, knock on the door facing the Black Stone, and say: "In the name of Allah, and by Allah, and to Allah, as He wills, and upon the religion of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace be upon him and his family. With the most excellent of Allah's names, praise be to Allah, and peace be upon the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon Muḥammad ibn ʿAbdullāh, peace be upon you, O Prophet, and Allah's mercy and blessings be upon you. Peace be upon the prophets of Allah

<!-- page 158 -->
and His messengers. Peace be upon Ibrāhīm and Ismāʿīl. Peace be upon the messengers, and upon the righteous servants of Allah. O Allah, send prayers upon Muḥammad and the family of Muḥammad, have mercy upon Muḥammad and the family of Muḥammad, and bless Muḥammad and the family of Muḥammad, as You sent prayers, mercy, and blessings upon Ibrāhīm and the family of Ibrāhīm in the worlds. Surely You are the Most Praiseworthy, the Most Glorious. O Allah, send prayers upon Muḥammad, Your servant and Your Prophet. Send prayers upon Ibrāhīm, Your intimate friend, and upon Your prophets and messengers. Open for me the doors of Your mercy, employ me in Your obedience and in what pleases You, and preserve me through the safekeeping of faith — You have not preserved me through the honour of Your face. Praise be to Allah who has made me among those who visit His House and among those who come to it. Upon all who have come to it and truly sought what they came for, may He fulfil their needs. O Allah, You are the best of those who are come to, and the best of those who grant requests. So I ask You by Allah, O Most Merciful, that You are Allah, there is no god but You, alone, with no partner, and that Muḥammad is Your servant and Your Prophet, and that You are One, Self-Sufficient, You did not beget and were not begotten, and none is equal to You. O Glorious One, O Majestic One, O Compassionate One, O Generous One — make Your finest gift to me the very first thing You give me: release me from the Fire."

Say "O Allah, release my neck from the Fire" three times, then add: "And expand for me a good and lawful provision, and protect me from the evil of the devils of jinn and men, and from the evil of the corrupt among Arabs and non-Arabs."

Then leave the house and raise your hands and say: "O Allah, I ask You in this my station, at the beginning of my rites, to accept my repentance, to forgive my sin, and to remove my burden from me. Praise be to Allah who has guided me to His Sacred House." Then proceed to the corner and receive the Black Stone if you are able and capable of reaching it. If you cannot reach it, place your hands upon it; and if you cannot manage that, point toward it with your hand. Then begin the ṭawāf.


<!-- page 159 -->
On the subject of circumambulation, Allah the Almighty said: "And let them go around the ancient house" [Al-Hajj: 29], and He the Almighty said: "And We charged Ibrahim and Isma'il to purify My house for those who circumambulate it, and those who perform prayer, and those who bow and prostrate."

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (peace be upon him) was asked about the origin of tawaf. He said that when Allah created Adam (peace be upon him), He commanded the angels to prostrate to him, and they were awed by what Allah had taught Adam of the names. They said to themselves: What could Allah the Almighty create more honored than us? And when Allah informed them that He would place a vicegerent on the earth, they said: Will You place therein one who will cause corruption and shed blood? In so saying they had objected to Allah the Almighty, and He said to them, glorified and exalted be He: "I know what you do not know" [Al-Baqarah: 30] — meaning what they had uttered — and: "I know what you reveal and what you conceal" [Al-Baqarah: 33] — meaning what they had said among themselves and what they had hidden in their hearts.

They came to recognize that they had committed a sin by objecting to the command of Allah regarding Adam (peace be upon him), so they took refuge at the Throne and circled it for seven rounds seeking to appease their Lord. He accepted their repentance and commanded them: Descend and build Me a house on earth, that those of My servants who disobey Me may circle it as you have circled My Throne, and I shall be as pleased with them as I have been pleased with you. So the angels built the house. The place where that house stood was the first site to be circumambulated by Adam (peace be upon him), and it remained so until it was raised up at the time of the Flood. Then Allah the Almighty revealed its location to Ibrahim, and he built the house upon its original foundation.

'Abbad ibn Bakr al-Basri said to Ja'far ibn Muhammad (peace be upon him), or the son of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him): We have received a narration from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and we wish to present it to you — if it is correct, we will accept it, and if it is false, we will leave it. He said: Recite what you have heard. They said: We have heard that the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: Whoever rises after the first portion of the night has passed, performs ablution, approaches the Black Stone,

<!-- page 160 -->
and testifies that there is no god but Allah and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah, then completes seven rounds and prays two rak'ahs in which he recites after the opening of the book Surah al-Kafirun and then Surah al-Ikhlas, in whichever mosque he wishes, then stands between the Black Stone and the door and praises Allah the Almighty as He deserves and sends prayers upon the Prophet and his household — his feet shall not move until Allah grants his petition and opens for him eight gates of Heaven that he may enter through whichever he wishes. Allah the Almighty shall record three thousand good deeds for him, erase three thousand bad deeds from him, and raise him three thousand degrees.

Abu 'Abdillah (peace be upon him) said: All of this is true and sound, and it is for six thousand. He then said: If tawaf begins at the house, the pilgrim should receive the Black Stone or touch it, or point toward it, and then commence the circumambulation, keeping the house to his left and going on his right. He should circumambulate seven rounds, beginning and ending at the Black Corner. Each complete circuit from the Black Corner back to it counts as one round, and he continues in this manner until he has completed seven rounds — performing three rounds at a quick pace and walking four at a normal pace. The quick pace is a brisk, vigorous walk, and this is what the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family commanded his companions to do, and so they did. He told them that the Quraysh had claimed that the companions of Muhammad had been weakened and exhausted by the fever of Yathrib, and so he wished to display to them the vigor and strength of his companions.

If the pilgrim leaves the quick pace in these three rounds, or in some of them, there is nothing on him. And there is no quick pace for women. In every tawaf around the house, the Black Corner and the Yemeni Corner should be received or touched if one is able to do so, or indicated toward if one cannot. The pilgrim should circumambulate from behind the Hijr — that is, he should not enter the Hijr; if he does so out of ignorance, he must re-enter and exit, and resume his tawaf from behind it. He should not receive the two corners that follow the Black Stone. Whoever cannot walk should circumambulate on a conveyance or be carried; circumambulating barefoot is preferable, though circumambulating in footwear is also permissible.

It is recommended to recite and supplicate during tawaf, and there is no harm in permissible speech during it. Among the supplications narrated from Ja'far ibn

<!-- page 161 -->
Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) to be said during circumambulation, he commanded that when one is beneath the water-spout of the Ka'bah, one should say: "O Allah, save me from the Fire." And when one is near the Black Stone, one should say: "O Allah, grant me good in this world and good in the hereafter, and protect me from the torment of the Fire." And when one is in the seventh round and has finished at the Black Stone, one should press against the house, placing one's belly and face against it, and say: "Praise be to Allah who has honored you, exalted you, and made you a gathering place for people and a place of safety. O Allah, this house is Your house, and the servant is Your servant, and this is the place of one who seeks Your protection from the Fire. O Allah, I am in need of You, I fear You, and with You I seek shelter. Do not alter my body, and do not change my name. O Allah, I am weak, so strengthen me in fulfilling Your pleasure in my weakness, and multiply my deeds and accept them from me. O Allah, grant me good in this world and in the hereafter, and save us from the torment of the Fire."

He (peace be upon him) said that there are angels who say "Amen" to the prayer of the believer.

One should not perform tawaf except in a state of ritual purity with ablution. If a person performs the obligatory tawaf without ablution, he must repeat the circumambulation. If he circumambulates voluntarily without ablution, there is no prayer for those two rak'ahs of tawaf and nothing else is required of him. If a person experiences a discharge or breaks his ablution during tawaf, he should perform ablution and wash the affected area; if it was a voluntary tawaf, he builds upon what he has completed even if the interruption occurred at the beginning; if it was the obligatory tawaf, and he has completed more than half, he builds upon it, and if he has completed half or less, he begins the tawaf again.

The obligatory tawaf consists of: the tawaf of 'Umrah upon first entering Mecca, then the tawaf of Hajj when one returns from Mina — which is the tawaf al-Ifadah — and both of these are accompanied by a sa'i between al-Safa and al-Marwah, which we shall describe in its proper place. Then comes the tawaf of women after the tawaf al-Ifadah and its accompanying sa'i, which is the farewell tawaf of the house. Each of these consists of seven rounds. The farewell tawaf upon departing Mecca is highly recommended, and all other circumambulations are voluntary and carry great virtue. One may circumambulate at any hour of the night or day, save for one week.

<!-- page 162 -->
He (peace be upon him) said: The menstruating woman and the woman in a state of lochia should complete all the rituals after she has washed and is purified, except for tawaf — she does not circumambulate until she is purified. The woman experiencing irregular bleeding may circumambulate and perform all the rituals, unless she is in a confirmed state of menstruation. The menstruating woman may perform the sa'i between al-Safa and al-Marwah once she is purified. Women should hasten their tawaf and their visit if they fear the onset of menstruation.

The Imams (peace be upon them) permitted those who are ill during tawaf or sa'i to rest, building upon what they have already completed of the tawaf and sa'i. If a person attends a congregational prayer during tawaf, he should interrupt his circumambulation at the point he has reached, pray the prayer, and then continue building upon it.

One should not perform the obligatory tawaf hastily without reason, nor should one perform the obligatory tawaf during a time when he believes a prayer time is about to enter, or when the imam's prayer is ongoing and he has not yet completed his tawaf and sa'i. Rather, he should delay the tawaf until after the obligatory prayer, then circumambulate. The Imams permitted the interruption of voluntary tawaf for a matter of righteousness that arises, and they narrated from al-Husayn ibn 'Ali (peace be upon him) that he was circumambulating the house voluntarily when a man came to him with a need. He interrupted his tawaf and went with the man. He was asked about this and said: How could I not go with him? The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: Whoever walks with his believing brother in his need, and even if the need is not fulfilled, it is written for the one who accompanied him as a reward equivalent to an 'Umrah; and if the need is fulfilled, it is written for him as a Hajj and an 'Umrah. I went with this man in his need and it was fulfilled, so I gained a Hajj and an 'Umrah — then I returned to my tawaf.

They said (peace be upon them): Whoever is overcome by sleep during the obligatory tawaf or sa'i and has not been able to complete it, if he has passed the halfway point he builds upon it, and if he has not passed the halfway point he begins again. If he leaves it until he rests and then completes it, there is nothing on him. If doubt arises during tawaf, he builds upon certainty. If a person increases in his circumambulation — for instance, performing eight rounds — those six extra rounds count as a second week's tawaf, and if this was the obligatory circumambulation, the first is obligatory and the second is voluntary,

<!-- page 163 -->
and he should pray four rak'ahs — two for each week — and perform the sa'i between al-Safa and al-Marwah.

Seven rounds of sa'i are required. If he wishes, he may perform another seven voluntarily; and if he wishes, he may leave the voluntary sa'i. It is not permissible deliberately to separate two weeks without praying two rak'ahs between them, except in the case of omission as we have mentioned.

When the pilgrim has completed a week of tawaf around the house, he should proceed to the Maqam of Ibrahim and position himself so that the Maqam is between him and the house, and pray two rak'ahs — for Allah the Almighty said: "And take from the Maqam of Ibrahim a place of prayer" [Al-Baqarah: 125]. No one should pray these rak'ahs except from behind the Maqam, keeping it between himself and the house.

Whoever performs the obligatory tawaf after 'Asr should pray the two rak'ahs of tawaf at that time. If he performs a voluntary tawaf, he should delay the two rak'ahs until after the disliked time has passed. If he performs a voluntary tawaf after Fajr, he does not pray the two rak'ahs until the time when nafl prayer is permissible — as we have mentioned in the chapters on prayer. However, if he circumambulates a tawaf of necessity, he prays the two rak'ahs when he finishes it at any time. It is recommended that he recite in the two rak'ahs of tawaf, after the opening of the book, Surah al-Kafirun in the first rak'ah and Surah al-Ikhlas in the second. All of this is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt (may Allah's prayers be upon them).

They said (peace be upon them): After he completes the two rak'ahs of tawaf and prays, he should return to the Black Stone and receive it, then go to Zamzam. He should drink from its water and pour some over himself if he is able, then go out through the gate of al-Safa, praise Allah, declare His oneness and His greatness, and pray for what is available to him, looking toward the house in the direction of the Black Corner.

It is narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him), traced back to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), that at al-Safa during the Farewell Pilgrimage he said: "There is no god but Allah alone, He has no partner; to Him belongs the dominion and to Him belongs all praise; He gives life and causes death, while He Himself is ever-living and does not die; in His hand is all good and He is capable of all things. There is no god but Allah alone; He has fulfilled His promise, aided His servant, and defeated the confederates alone." He said this three times.

Abu 'Abdillah (peace be upon him) would say after that: "O Allah, I ask You for forgiveness and well-being in

<!-- page 164 -->
this world and in the hereafter. I ask You to send prayers upon Muhammad and his good household, to strengthen me in acting upon what You have taught me of Your right, to make me content with what You have provided for me of Your sustenance, and I ask You, O Allah, for good in this world and good in the hereafter, and I ask You to protect me from the torment of the Fire."

He (peace be upon him) said: When you have finished your supplication upon al-Safa, descend and walk at a measured, tranquil pace. When you descend into the valley at the point marked by the first beacon — which is the flag marking the beginning of the striving — quicken your pace until you ascend from the belly of the valley and reach the second beacon, which is likewise a flag marking the end of the striving. It is for this reason that this section is called the sa'i between al-Safa and al-Marwah.

The first person to perform the sa'i was Ibrahim (peace be upon him). When he descended from al-Safa and reached the belly of the valley, Satan blocked his path, and Ibrahim hastened to flee from him until he was stopped at the far edge of the valley. The accursed one then turned away from him, so Ibrahim continued on his way.

He (peace be upon him) said: It is recommended to say during the sa'i: "In the name of Allah, Allah is the greatest, and may Allah send prayers upon Muhammad and his family. O Allah, forgive, have mercy, and overlook what You know — for You are the Most Mighty, the Most Generous." When he leaves the point of quick pacing and reaches the place where he walks at a normal pace, he should raise his hands toward the sky and say: "O Lord of munificence, bounty, sublimity, blessings, and goodness — my deeds are weak, so accept them from me and send prayers upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, for he is his family and they are his family."

He (peace be upon him) said: Women are not required to quicken their pace; they walk throughout. The man quickens his pace, then ascends to al-Marwah and says what was said upon al-Safa, then returns to al-Safa, stands upon it likewise, and returns to al-Marwah, continuing in this manner until he has completed seven rounds — beginning with al-Safa and ending with al-Marwah. He supplicates and prays as described above at each mount. If he begins with al-Marwah before al-Safa, he must discard that round and begin again with al-Safa, ending with al-Marwah — because the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family, when he ascended al-Safa, recited the verse: "Indeed al-Safa and al-Marwah are among the rites of Allah" [Al-Baqarah: 158], and then said: "Begin with what Allah has begun with." This is how the one who circumambulates the house should perform the sa'i.


<!-- page 165 -->
The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) did likewise, and if he broke it into portions due to some excuse, there is no harm in that — whether he needed to eat and rest, faced time constraints, or was offered something he could not decline. He should not delay it without an excuse, as has been stated regarding the one who begins the sa'i in a composed and unhurried manner.

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) was asked whether the sa'i between al-Safa and al-Marwah is obligatory, and he said, "Yes." It was then asked whether shortening the prayer while travelling is also obligatory, and he said, "Yes." When it was said to him, "But Allah Almighty said: 'And when you travel in the land, there is no blame upon you if you shorten the prayer if you fear that those who disbelieve may harm you. Verily, the disbelievers are ever to you a clear enemy.'" [al-Nisa: 101] — and that the one who performs Hajj or 'Umrah has no blame in performing the sa'i with them — he invoked the remembrance of Allah Almighty.

This is recorded in His book, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family acted upon it and commanded it, and it is he upon whom Allah Almighty imposed the clarification, saying: "And We have sent down to you the Reminder so that you may make clear to the people what has been sent down to them." [al-Nahl: 44] He made it obligatory upon the people to accept it, saying: "And whatever the Messenger gives you, take it; and whatever he forbids you, refrain from it." [al-Hashr: 7]

The verse "Indeed, al-Safa and al-Marwah are among the rites of Allah. So whoever performs Hajj or 'Umrah — there is no blame upon him for walking between them" [al-Baqarah: 158] is evidence that performing the sa'i between them is obligatory. For if it were merely permissible, it would suffice to say "there is no blame upon him for not walking between them." The statement of Allah Almighty that there is "no blame" upon the one who performs the sa'i came only after it was seen that some held it to be blameworthy. So Allah Almighty clarified that it is among His rites which He has ordained, and that there is no sin upon those who perform it voluntarily — for it was said that the Ansar used to come to Manat,

<!-- page 166 -->
following the example of al-Qadir, and they were reluctant to perform the sa'i between al-Safa and al-Marwah. Allah Almighty informed them that there is no blame upon them in that, and that it is among His rites. Likewise, the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) commanded the shortening of the prayer while travelling — some Muslims found this difficult and it weighed upon them to do so — and Allah Almighty told them that there is no blame upon them in it. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: whoever deliberately abandons the sa'i must perform Hajj anew upon encountering it, and if he forgot it, he is commanded to make it up, just as one who forgets the two rak'ahs of tawaf is commanded to make them up.

**Remembrance of the one who combines 'Umrah with Hajj:**

Ja'far ibn Muhammad said — may Allah's prayers and blessings be upon him — regarding the one who combines 'Umrah with Hajj, meaning whoever brings them together in one intention and enters ihram for both together and forbids for himself and his family all that we mentioned in the chapter on ihram: if the one performing tamattu' circumambulates the Ka'bah and performs the sa'i between al-Safa and al-Marwah, intending by that tawaf and sa'i the 'Umrah, then he has fulfilled it and should release himself from ihram, taking from his hair and nails. He remains in this lawful state due to an excuse, and so too was the command of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) to those who were with him in the Farewell Pilgrimage — those who had no hady with them were released from ihram when they completed the tawaf and sa'i, and they became fully released. But the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family, having brought the hady, did not dissolve their ihram. The 'Umrah was not merged into the Hajj, because Allah says: "And do not shave your heads until the hady reaches its destination." [al-Baqarah: 196] This means until the hady is slaughtered. But Allah Almighty sent down the command of tamattu' at that time, and he said: "Had I known from the outset what I later came to know, I would not have brought the hady." He encouraged tamattu', and the people did not know this — they had only known 'Umrah to be performed separately.

The Commander of the Believers, 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (may Allah's prayers be upon him), arrived on that day from Yemen, driving

<!-- page 167 -->
a gift of hady. He found Fatimah (peace be upon her) in Hajj with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family; she had put on dyed garments and had come out of ihram, and they disapproved of that for her. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) had commanded her to do so, and he brought the matter to him and informed him of it. He said, "Yes. And you, O 'Ali — for what did you make your ihram?" He said, "I said: O Allah, I make ihram for what Your Messenger made ihram." He said, "Do not dissolve your ihram until you have sacrificed the hady; complete your 'Umrah and remain in your ihram until you are released from Hajj." He shared with him (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) and his family in the hady, and what he brought from Yemen was combined with what the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) brought, making him a partner in all of it.

Abu 'Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was asked about the one performing tamattu' — how should he cut his hair? He said he should not shave his head with a razor, but should shorten it or take from his limbs, and leave it for the pilgrimage. It was asked: what if he shaved? He said: if he shaved and his hair did not grow back between that time and the day of sacrifice, then he owes blood — a sheep which he slaughters and distributes its meat in charity. He said: the razor passes over his head on the day of sacrifice whether his hair has grown back or not. The same applies to one who has no hair on his head — the razor passes over him where the head is located.

If the one performing tamattu' forgets to shorten his hair until he has entered ihram for Hajj and then remembers, he should seek Allah's forgiveness and nothing more is required of him. He said: whoever is released from his tamattu' may travel between it and entering ihram for Hajj as he wishes, but he should not perform voluntary tawaf after the tamattu' tawaf until he has dissolved his ihram and shortened his hair.

When the one performing tamattu' has completed his 'Umrah, he should not wear [ihram] garments nor resemble those in ihram. The people of Makkah should likewise be in a state appropriate to the days of Hajj, and if they do not perform Hajj they remain in their ordinary state. If the mahram man approaches his wife before she has shortened her hair, he owes a sheep; if he does so after she has shortened, he owes nothing. Once what was forbidden in ihram is resolved upon release from it, and if the one performing tamattu' arrives in Makkah on the day of Tarwiyah, he performs the tawaf of his 'Umrah and the sa'i and walks, then enters ihram for Hajj on that day. The ruling regarding women

<!-- page 168 -->
is: if a woman fears that menstruation will prevent her from performing the tawaf and sa'i of Hajj before departing for Hajj, having combined 'Umrah with Hajj in tamattu', and she menstruates before performing the tawaf of 'Umrah until she becomes pure — then she should perform tawaf before the tawaf of Hajj. He said: there is no tamattu' for the people of Makkah, but Allah Almighty has permitted it for those who come from distant lands for Hajj, and this is the meaning of the saying of the Most High: "That is for those whose family is not present at the Sacred Mosque." [al-Baqarah: 196] This is because the people of Makkah may perform 'Umrah whenever they wish.

He said: whoever enters the Sacred Precinct and performs 'Umrah in one of the months of Hajj, then remains until he performs Hajj, that is tamattu'. And the one who performs tamattu' — as Allah Almighty said: "So he did not get away with it" [al-Baqarah: 196] — owes a sheep. He slaughters it in the days of Mina and distributes its meat in charity, eating none of it himself. If he does not find a sheep, he participates in a camel, and we will explain the required share of the camel in the section on the hady. If he does not find that, then he fasts — as Allah Almighty said — three days during Hajj: one day before Tarwiyah, the day of Tarwiyah, and the day of 'Arafah; and seven days when he returns to his family. If he fasts them on the way back, that suffices him.
