If he did not fast the three days during Hajj nor on the way back — whether out of ignorance or forgetfulness — then he leaves them and fasts ten days upon returning to his family. Fasting them continuously is better. When the day of Tarwiyah comes, the one performing tamattu' enters ihram for Hajj from the Sacred Mosque, just as one enters ihram from the miqat; the people of Makkah enter ihram from their houses rather than from the miqat outside Makkah, and whoever resides in Makkah without being of its people enters ihram likewise from the place where they reside.

**Mention of going out to Mina and standing at 'Arafah:**

Ja'far ibn Muhammad said — may Allah's prayers be upon him — to one of those whom he was teaching the rituals, among those who were performing tamattu': on the day of Tarwiyah, Allah willing, bathe, put on your garments, and enter

<!-- page 169 -->
the Grand Mosque barefoot, with serenity and dignity. Perform seven rounds of voluntary tawaf if you wish upon arrival. When the sun passes its zenith, perform the obligatory prayer, then enter ihram for Hajj and do as was done at Dhu al-Hulayfah. Then go out to Mina.

He said: the people should leave Makkah for Mina on the day of Tarwiyah from the beginning of the day until its end. It was called the day of Tarwiyah because the people would water their animals on it, as there was no water in Mina at that time. This is the eighth day of Dhu al-Hijjah. The people went out from Makkah to Mina — those who wished to perform Hajj — some riding and some walking, and those who could not find a mount walked.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad said (may Allah's prayers be upon him): riding for those who are able to do so is better than walking, because the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) went out riding. Jabir ibn 'Abdullah said: "I looked out at the company of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) that day — people on foot and people riding, on his right and on his left and ahead of him and behind him, both mounted and on foot." When they reached the foot of the general path, the pedestrians lined up before him. They were gathered at a resting place. He passed by them and they said: "O Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family, we have been travelling a long time and walking has exhausted us." He said: "May Allah reward you," and urged them, saying: "If you use a trot — meaning a light quick pace — your bodies will feel lighter and the ground will pass beneath you more quickly." They did so and it eased them. The rider is stronger for the standing at 'Arafah and for supplication, so riding is recommended for those who are able and capable of it.

It is narrated that, when the season comes, the imam should not pray the noon prayer on the day of Tarwiyah except in Mina, and he sleeps the night there. Then the people go out from Mina to 'Arafah the following day — the day of 'Arafah — after the sun has risen. He said: it is recommended to bathe on that day for one who fears that fasting may weaken him during the standing and supplication at 'Arafah. When they arrive at 'Arafah they descend there, and it is recommended to descend and stand close to

the mountain. Going down and standing when one becomes aware of it is forbidden. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said:

<!-- page 170 -->
those of discernment who descend below it and stand there have no valid pilgrimage. All of 'Arafah is a valid standing-place.

He said: when the sun passes its zenith, the gathering assembles. The imam comes to the floor of the valley and addresses the people, then gives permission to the muezzin who calls the adhan for the noon prayer. The imam leads the people in the noon prayer, then the muezzins call the iqamah and the people pray the afternoon prayer with him — combining the two prayers with one adhan and two iqamahs. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) did likewise.

Then he stands and the people stand, facing the sun with their faces, supplicating to Allah Almighty and expressing their desire for Him and asking Him their needs for this world and the Hereafter, until the sun sets. Whoever wishes recites the Qur'an, and whoever wishes glorifies Allah, praises Him, and seeks His forgiveness.

It is narrated from Muhammad ibn 'Ali ibn al-Husayn (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that when he stood at 'Arafah, he would open Surah al-Baqarah. After reciting ten verses he would glorify Allah, praise Him, seek forgiveness, and supplicate, then open the next ten verses, doing likewise until he completed the surah.

It is narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that he said: when standing at 'Arafah, praise Allah and glorify Him, and pray upon the Prophet Muhammad and his family, then say: "O Allah, I ask You by You — by Your power, Your presence, Your generosity, Your grace — and from You. You are the Hearer of those who hear, the Seer of those who see, the Swift in reckoning, the Ruler of rulers, and the Most Merciful of the merciful. Pray upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad and grant me what You deem fitting." Then raise your hands and face toward the sky and say: "O Allah, my need is such that if You grant it, it will not harm me, and if You withhold it, it will not benefit me. What You have given me I ask You for — release my neck from the Fire. O Allah, I am Your servant; I have placed my trust in You and I am grateful for knowing You. I ask You to help me until You are satisfied with me, and to make me among those whose deeds and striving were accepted." Then say: praise be to Allah for His forbearance after His knowledge; praise be to Allah for His pardon after His power; praise be to Allah for His mercy that preceded His wrath; and praise be to Allah with all praise for all His blessings. Continue until you conclude: praise be to Allah for what Allah loves and accepts; praise be to Allah for His countless blessings that can never be fully recompensed. O Allah, I ask You —


<!-- page 171 -->
By your beautiful names, by your strength, your power, your majesty, and by all that your knowledge encompasses, and by the truth of your prophet, and by your great and most exalted name — he who calls upon you by it, you are truly bound to answer him — we ask you to forgive us all our sins, all that you know of us. Then ask God for your needs in this life and the next, and say often: "O God, I ask you to release my neck from the Fire." Repeat this prayer and others, whatever is opened to you, and grow in supplication and rejoicing and seeking forgiveness until the sun sets. When it sets, say: "O God, do not make this the last covenant between me and this station. Open for me in it what you have never withheld from me. Turn me out of it successful, my prayers answered, my guilt forgiven, returned with the best return of any member of your delegation to you and your pilgrims. Make me today among those you honor in your delegation, and grant me the best of what you have granted any of them in goodness and blessing, with mercy, contentment, and wellness. Bless me in what I return to of family and wealth, few and many, and bless them. O God, expand upon us from your good and lawful provision, and do not deprive me, and do not draw away from me the evil of jinn and mankind. Send blessings upon Muhammad and the family of Muhammad, as you are his Lord and they belong to you."

It was said, peace be upon him, that whoever fell ill and fainted was stood beside in a station that he knew, and it was said: it is not right to stand at ʿArafah except in a state of purity. It was narrated from the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, that he said: "The most wretched of the people of ʿArafah is the one who departs from it believing he has not been forgiven." Abu Jaʿfar said: Muhammad ibn ʿAli, may Allah's prayers be upon him — meaning the one who despairs of God's mercy. And Abu Jaʿfar Muhammad ibn ʿAli, may Allah's prayers be upon him, once stood at ʿArafah and said: "Allah is generous, but Allah does not overwhelm." On the evening of this station, both the righteous and the sinful receive a share from God; the righteous will be answered for his hereafter and this world, while the sinful will be given his portion and his lot from what he asked of this world. Al-Husayn ibn ʿAli, peace be upon him, saw a man asking from the people on the day of ʿArafah and said, and rebuked him: "Do you ask from other than God

<!-- page 172 -->
on this day? It is a day for those in the wombs seeking their sustenance." Abu Jaʿfar ibn Muhammad ʿAli, peace be upon him, would not turn away anyone on the day of ʿArafah who came asking. And Jaʿfar ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, used to increase on that day in supplication, prayer, charity, and seeking forgiveness.

— Mention of the Departure from ʿArafah to Muzdalifah:

Allah Almighty said: "Then flow on from where the people flow on." Muhammad, may God's prayers be upon him: the Quraysh used to depart from Muzdalifah in the pre-Islamic era, saying: "We are the foremost among the people of the House." So Allah Almighty commanded them to depart from ʿArafah, from where the people depart. The people had not been standing at ʿArafah before; they were standing at the Sacred Mosque. So they were commanded to stand at ʿArafah and depart from it. One does not depart from ʿArafah until the sun has set and the disc has disappeared. When that occurs, the people proceed to Muzdalifah, and they do not pray Maghrib until they arrive there, combining Maghrib with ʿIsha at the time of ʿIsha with the imam, with one call to prayer and two iqāmahs. They remain in Muzdalifah on the night of the Day of Sacrifice, and the best place is near the sacred mosque. Whoever departs from ʿArafah before the sun sets must offer a sacrifice or give its value in charity. He should increase upon departing in the remembrance of Allah Almighty and in seeking forgiveness. Allah Almighty says: "Then flow on from where the people flow on, and seek forgiveness from Allah; indeed Allah is Forgiving, Merciful." [al-Baqarah: 199]. The departure is to be made with tranquility. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, and his family, when he departed from ʿArafah in the Farewell Pilgrimage, was riding his camel and held her by the reins until her head nearly touched his leg, and he gestured with his right hand saying: "O people, tranquility, tranquility." And all that descended from the mountains moved at a gentle pace until they reached Muzdalifah, where he combined the two evening prayers. Abu Abdullah was asked

<!-- page 173 -->
about Jaʿfar ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, regarding the combining of the two prayers before reaching Muzdalifah, and he said: "No." If one went a third of the night and did so deliberately, blood is required of him. He also said: spending the night at Muzdalifah on the night of the Day of Sacrifice is a confirmed Sunnah. Whoever abandons it deliberately, knowing it is from the Sunnah, owes a sacrifice — unless he returns for the rest of the night and stands there. If he abandoned it out of ignorance, and it has passed, then having stood in Muzdalifah even partially suffices, and nothing is upon him. It is recommended for the imam to stand in Muzdalifah at al-Quzaḥ, which is the mountain upon which the stand is made. All of Muzdalifah is a valid standing place. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, permitted women and the weak to depart from Muzdalifah at night, to throw the jamarāt at night, and to pray Fajr in Minā. However, the imam and the rest of the people do not depart from Muzdalifah until they have prayed Fajr. Jaʿfar ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, stood and was brought to Muzdalifah until the sun shone on Thabīr, which is the first mountain upon which the sun rises. When he saw it, he said: "Shine, O Thabīr! Shine, O Thabīr! I say as my brother Moses said: 'My Lord, expand for me my breast, make my matter easy for me, and loosen the knot from my tongue, that they may understand my speech; and appoint for me a minister from my family — my brother — reinforce me through him and let him share in my affair.'" Jaʿfar ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, also said: when you stand in the Sacred Mosque, praise Allah and glorify him, and recite from the Quran whatever you are able, then send blessings upon the Prophet and his family, then supplicate for yourself as much as you are able. For the Messenger of God, when he prayed Fajr on the Day of Sacrifice, gathered together in supplication until the sacred boundary became distinct from him. He faced the qibla, glorifying and praising God alone, and continued standing thus until he departed from Muzdalifah toward Minā. He came to a valley between

Muzdalifah and Minā, moved a little, and took the middle road that leads out toward the Great Jamrah.

<!-- page 174 -->
Until he reached the jamrah that was at the tree and pelted it with seven pebbles. Then he came to Minā, slaughtered, and shaved. It was said: the saʿy in the Sunnah should be performed as the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and his family, performed it — throwing the Jamrat al-ʿAqabah on that day before slaughtering, then slaughtering and shaving, or cutting the hair, whichever seemed appropriate.

— Mention of Throwing the Jamarāt:

Jaʿfar ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: the pebbles of the jamarāt may be taken from Muzdalifah and from other places. Whoever is in Minā should not transfer pebbles from one jamrah to another; rather, the pebbles for all the jamarāt are taken from one place. It is recommended that the pebbles be the size used for throwing, approximately the size of a chickpea, and it is disliked to use broken stones. It is recommended to wash the pebbles before throwing them. The one throwing the jamrah should stand with Minā to his right and the House to his left when he intends to throw, and he should throw from the middle of the valley, not from above. Abu Jaʿfar ibn ʿAli, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: when you throw the jamarāt, say the takbīr with each pebble thrown. Jaʿfar ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: whoever throws the Jamrat al-ʿAqabah on the Day of Sacrifice before slaughtering, then comes to Minā and slaughters and shaves, then proceeds to the House and circumambulates it seven times and performs saʿy between al-Ṣafā and al-Marwah seven times as well — and this is the circumambulation of Ḥajj — then returns and performs the circumambulation of the women; if he has done all of that, everything becomes permissible to him that was forbidden to the muhrim by reason of his ihrām. He then returns to Minā and remains there during the days of Tashrīq, throwing the jamarāt each day after the sun declines. Each jamrah is pelted with seven pebbles, beginning with the lesser jamrah, then the middle, then the major. He has some latitude to throw at any point during the day. It is desirable that he walk to the jamarāt, as the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, used to walk to the jamarāt when he threw them.

<!-- page 175 -->
It is required to send blessings upon him when throwing the jamarāt, whether walking or riding, and there is no hardship or restriction in that. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, and his family held that throwing the jamarāt should be done by night, and whoever misses it during the day throws it by night. Whoever places the pebbles of one jamrah upon another should repeat the throwing, starting with the lesser jamrah and ending with the major — which is the Jamrat al-ʿAqabah. Whoever misses any of the throwing of the jamarāt must make it up if it was missed deliberately or by inadvertence; and whoever hastens in two days may leave the remaining pebbles with him — he should not throw them again at the jamarāt after departing.

— Mention of the Sacrificial Offering (Hady):

Allah Almighty said: "They are the ones who disbelieved and turned you away from the Sacred Mosque, and the sacrificial offerings from reaching their place of slaughter." [al-Fatḥ: 25]. And He said, Blessed and Exalted: "Do not shave your heads until the sacrificial offering reaches its place of slaughter." [al-Baqarah: 196]. The place for the sacrificial offering in a single Ḥajj that is combined with ʿUmrah is Minā, and for a single ʿUmrah, it is in Mecca. When the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, presented his gift, he said: "This is the one who is being offered, and every abode of offering is its place of slaughter; so the people slaughter at their stations." It is desirable that the man slaughter his own sacrificial offering himself, or at least place his hand alongside the hand of the one doing the slaughtering. If he is unable, he should stand beside it, and the butcher will act as his agent. The offering should be given only to a Muslim. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, and his family associated ʿAli, peace be upon him, in his sacrificial offering. All the offerings that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, brought and that ʿAli, peace be upon him, brought from Yemen totaled one hundred camels. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, slaughtered sixty-three of them with his own hand, while the remainder stood upright and were presented to him. He commanded ʿAli to pray over them, and ʿAli slaughtered the rest. Then he commanded

<!-- page 176 -->
that a piece from each be placed in a pot and cooked. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him, ate of that flesh and drank from its broth, so that he might partake of each of them. Allah Almighty has mentioned that whoever brings a sacrificial offering to the south of it should eat from it. It is required to send blessings upon Allah when slaughtering a gift or a sacrificial animal in the state of iḥrām. He says: "I have turned my face toward the one who created the heavens and the earth, as a sincere Muslim, and I am not of the polytheists. My prayer, my devotion, my life, and my death are for Allah, the Lord of the worlds — no partner has He — and this is what I have been commanded, and I am of the Muslims. O God, this is from you and to you. In the name of God." Then he lays hold of it. The recitation of the name is part of the act, and it is not permissible to omit it except for one who forgets; if he remembers, he names God. Whoever cannot find a sacrificial offering on his own should share with others in whatever he is able, and the more he does so the better. The best sacrificial offering is the female camel, then the male camel, then female cattle. It was also narrated from ʿAli, may Allah's prayers be upon him, that female cattle are preferable to their males. Then come the males of cattle, then the ram from the sheep, which is better than the ewe, the ewe being one whose females are restrained or satisfied. The ewe is better than the thamm, which is the hornless female, which in turn is better than the naʿjah, and the naʿjah is better than the stallion; and the stallion is better than the female goat, and so on. None of these qualify except the mature and sound animal — except the sheep, whose horn may be cut off from the outside only — but not the salt-colored, which is one whose whiteness is mixed with black in places, such that the muzzle is black, and the extremities of the hands and feet, and the end of the tail. Before this was described the ram that was sent down to Ibrāhīm, peace be upon him. It is recommended to offer sacrifices that are robust and fat, and it is disliked to offer lean animals unless one purchased them fat and they became thin through no deliberate fault of his own. The scholars forbade the broken-horned animal — meaning one whose horn is broken all the way through — even if only the outer part is broken, as it is the most apparent defect. They directed that the eye and ear be examined carefully, meaning, peace be upon them, that there be no defect. They forbade the animal whose udder teats are severed — meaning the nipples of the udder.


<!-- page 177 -->
Regarding the jarba, the elderly animal, and the trunk — meaning the ear that has been cut off — they also disapproved of the mutabarri'a, which is the animal whose ears have been cut off from the front, and the mudabara, which is the animal whose hindquarters have been cut off, and the sharqa', which is the animal whose ear has been split in two, and the muqabbala, which has a round hole in its ears. As for a defect that befalls the sacrificial animal after it has been purchased and dedicated as a gift, nothing is required of the owner; he may reward it and accept its condition or find a substitute.

The marking of camels and cattle consists of stabbing their hump from the right side with an iron implement until blood flows, so that the animal is known as a sacrificial gift should it go astray. An ox is marked similarly, as is a sheep, if it has been dedicated and honored as a gift. When the animal is slaughtered, its meat is given in charity. If the sacrificial animal is damaged before it arrives at its destination, it is slaughtered where it stands, dipped in its own blood, marked, and left so that those who pass by may recognize it as a permissible gift — they may cut from it and eat as they wish. If it is a voluntary gift, the owner may eat from it and feed whomever he wishes, and nothing further is obligatory from it. What is obligatory does not apply to vows or rewards. If hunting or any act of corruption is involved in the Hajj, then nothing of it may be eaten, and the gift may not be sold — whether it is obligatory or otherwise.

Whoever damages his gift before it reaches its destination and finds no replacement, even if it goes astray, must purchase another in a different place. If the gift is the obligatory one, he must mark it or dedicate it and slaughter it. If it is not obligatory, then the matter is by choice. If a guide misleads the animal together with the one who purchased it, its owner may take it at will and need not be compensated for what was slaughtered; he may seek the price at his discretion from whoever sold it to him. Whoever finds a lost gift should make known what he has found. If he cannot find anyone to slaughter it by the end of the days of Tashreeq, he should inform its companion. Whoever has his gift slaughtered and then has parts of it stolen is required to present the gift — meaning to take it from Mina to Arafah and stand with it there, at Muzdalifah, and at all the ritual stations — and then slaughter it. Whoever slaughters a voluntary gift may eat from it, give charity from it, and feed from it whatever he wishes; there is no fixed timing for this. Abu 'Abd Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's blessings be upon him) was asked about the words of Allah the Almighty: *"So eat of it and feed"*

<!-- page 178 -->
*the poor and the wretched"* — and about His saying: *"eat of it and feed the contented and the needy."* The poor man is one who does not beg of people, and the wretched is in a worse condition than him and faces greater hardship. The contented one is he who is satisfied with what is given to him, whether much or little — he does not become angry, does not cry out, and does not twist in agitation. The poor man who passes by is distinct from these. Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: People are tested by this so that the contented may be distinguished from others. If a questioner stands before him, he is commanded to give him the head of the sacrificial animal as a gift. If the questioner takes it and passes by with joy, he is recognized as content. If he becomes angry, he is given from al-mulham — the cooked portions — and if his anger departs and he leaves satisfied, it becomes clear that he is not truly content.

They disliked — may peace be upon them — that anything of the sacrificial animals should leave Mina, except small amounts taken for a necessary purpose, such as something from the hump for medicinal use, or some of the hide, wool, hair, or sinew — the kinds of things needed during travel. They favored giving the hides of the sacrificial animals and offerings in charity, while permitting their use, and allowing the stomach lining to be given from them and from the proceeds. They forbade the sacrifice of the majalla animal — the one that has been honored and set apart, fed in its stall, and whose milk is withheld from being sold until it has been cleared of suspicion: camels for forty days, cattle for twenty days, sheep for seven days, ducks for five days, and chickens for three days.

They forbade riding the sacrificial animal in a manner that would harm or diminish it, or loading it with burdens or occupying it with work. What does it no harm is permitted, and the animal must not be beaten in any case. Whoever forgets to slaughter in Mina should slaughter in Makkah when he remembers. The days of Mina are three days following the day of sacrifice. The day after the day of sacrifice is the first of these, and it is called the Day of Qarr, because one settles in Mina and does not depart from it, nor leave it except to visit the House and perform the circumambulation. The second day is the Day of al-Nafr al-Awwal, as Allah the Almighty said: *"Whoever hastens in two days, there is no sin upon him"* — meaning hastening one's departure from Mina. The third day is the last of the days of the throwing.

<!-- page 179 -->
**Mention of the shaving and shortening:**

Allah the Almighty said: *"Allah has fulfilled His Messenger's vision in truth — you will surely enter the Grand Mosque, Allah willing, in safety, with heads shaved and hair shortened"* [Al-Fath: 27]. The shaving is not done with a razor alone, and the shortening is not accomplished merely by cutting the ends of the hair with scissors. It has been narrated from the Ahl al-Bayt (may Allah's prayers be upon them) that shaving is superior to shortening, and that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) shaved some of the pilgrims during the Farewell Pilgrimage and shortened the hair of others. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "O Allah, have mercy on those who shave." One who heard him asked, "And those who shorten, O Messenger of Allah?" He said: "O Allah, have mercy on those who shave and those who shorten." They ordered — may peace be upon them — that the shaving include taking from the sides of the beard and the mustache, and cutting the nails, as well as reaching with the razor to the bones exposed beneath the temples near the ear passages. We have mentioned that the bald man and the one with a shaved head each pass the razor over their head. The woman, upon exiting ihram, cuts some of the ends of her hair and does not shave her head or any part of it. Whoever forgot to shave or did so out of ignorance and then departed from Mina should shave wherever he recalls it or learns of the obligation, even if he is near Mina — he should return to it to shave. If he has already departed and traveled far from it, he should send his hair to Mina and have it thrown there if he is able to do so. They ordered that what falls of the hair be buried. It has been narrated from 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that he said: "Bury this hair, for everything that falls from the son of Adam belongs to him and should be buried." 'Ali ibn al-Husayn (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) used to bury his hair in a depression in Mina when he shaved, and at that time he would say: "O Allah, grant me light on the Day of Resurrection for every hair."

<!-- page 180 -->
**Mention of what the pilgrim does in Mina:**

Allah the Almighty said: *"Then let them fulfill their vows and circumambulate the Ancient House"* [Al-Hajj: 29]. It has been narrated from 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that he said: The throwing, the shaving, the fulfilling of vows, the walking, the sacrifices, and the circumambulation of the House of emancipation — which is the Tawaf al-Ziyara, also called Tawaf al-Ifadah, the Tawaf of Hajj — I have already mentioned this in the chapter on Tawaf. On the day of sacrifice, the pilgrims visit the House after they have shaved and slaughtered in Mina; they return to the House and circumambulate it, then return to Mina. Everything was permitted to them from that point, as I have mentioned, and it does not matter in which order one begins — whether with shaving, slaughtering, or throwing. It is recommended to hasten the Tawaf al-Ifadah on the day of sacrifice for the one performing tamattu'. They permitted the qarin and the mufrid to delay it. It is permissible to throw the jamarat before shaving and slaughtering; after both of these, however, one must not perform the visit before having shaved, slaughtered, and thrown, unless this was done in a state of forgetfulness or ignorance — if so, nothing is required of him, and he should then shave, slaughter, and throw. Whoever fails to spend the nights in Mina deliberately incurs a blood penalty for each night, even if this was done out of ignorance or forgetfulness — in the latter case, nothing is upon him, and Allah forgives them.

They observed that the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) shortened his prayer in Mina and commanded that Allah the Almighty be remembered abundantly during the days of Mina. Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) commented on the words of Allah the Almighty: *"When you have fulfilled your rituals, remember Allah as you remember your fathers, or with even greater remembrance"* [Al-Baqarah: 200]. He said: When the polytheists would settle in Mina, they would boast of their fathers and recount the glories of their ancestors. Allah the Almighty therefore commanded the Muslims to replace this with the remembrance of the Almighty. They disapproved — may peace be upon them — of fasting on the day of the sacrifice, the days of Tashreeq, and they said that when the pilgrim throws the Jamarat al-'Aqaba al-Kubra on the day of sacrifice, he is released from

<!-- page 181 -->
everything that was forbidden to him in the state of ihram, except hunting, women, and dwelling outside of Mina. If he then performs the circumambulation of the House and the sa'y between al-Safa and al-Marwah, everything that was forbidden to him in the state of ihram becomes permissible — except women. Once he performs the Tawaf al-Nisa', everything becomes permissible to him. They said that the takbir during the days of Mina is to be said after every obligatory prayer: the worshipper says it after the taslim and before rising, saying: "Allahu Akbar, Allahu Akbar, la ilaha ill-Allah, Allahu Akbar, Allahu Akbar, wa lillahi al-hamd — Allah is great for what He has guided us to, greater than what He has bestowed upon us of the sacrificial animals." He may visit the House each day if he wishes and perform voluntary circumambulation as much as he wishes, departing from it during the day and returning to his dwelling in Mina.

**Mention of the departure from Mina:**

Allah the Almighty said: *"And remember Allah during the appointed days. Whoever hastens in two days, there is no sin upon him, and whoever delays, there is no sin upon him — for the one who fears Allah"* [Al-Baqarah: 203]. Abu 'Abd Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: This refers to the stay in Mina — if you wish to remain, you have done well, and if you wish to hasten your departure in two days, you may do so, as Allah the Almighty has said. I have already mentioned the days of Tashreeq and the departure, from Abu 'Abd Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's blessings be upon him): whoever hastens his departure on the second day of Tashreeq — which is the third day after the day of sacrifice — should not depart until he has prayed the noon prayer and thrown the jamarat; thereafter he may depart if he wishes, between then and sunset. If the sun sets, he is considered among the people of the third day and may depart whenever he wishes, from the beginning of the day until after he has prayed Fajr and thrown the jamarat. Upon departing from Mina, it is mentioned that one should take the road toward Makkah before the first departure. It is recommended for those who depart to descend at al-Muhassab — which is al-Bat-ha' — and rest there for a time before continuing, as the Messenger of Allah

<!-- page 182 -->
(may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) descended there when the journey came, rested for an hour, and then departed. It was said: Whoever is among the first to depart may remain in Makkah until the people have shaved, and if the people are departing for Makkah, one may enter the House — whoever desires to enter it should do so if able. Whoever does not enter, nothing is required of him. Abu 'Abd Allah Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: If you are able to enter the House, enter it whenever you wish; but if you fear the crowd, do not endanger yourself. He then mentioned accounts of people who perished in the crowd within it.

He said: It is recommended for whoever wishes to enter the Kaaba to perform a ritual bath. Upon entering, after praising Allah for what is due to Him and sending prayers upon the Prophet and his family, one says: "O Allah, You said — and whoever enters it is safe — so protect me from Your punishment, cleanse my heart of arrogance, hypocrisy, ostentation, and doubt regarding Your religion, and shield me from the Fire on the Day of Resurrection. O Allah, whoever has prepared, equipped, or made ready in order to benefit a creature — grant him what he seeks and let his reward be from You, O Lord. My own preparation and readiness — grant its reward from You alone; do not disappoint my hope today, and do not turn me away today in disappointment from Your House. You have made it sacred, and You are the Most Generous of those whose house is visited. O Most Great, there is no god but You."

He said: Whoever wishes to pray voluntarily inside the House should do so, and it is recommended to pray between the two pillars on the red marble — for there is no spot better on earth than it — as the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) prayed there. He then rose and faced each corner of the House, offering a prayer at it, before returning to the red marble to pray upon it. He said: One does not pray an obligatory prayer inside the Kaaba, and it is not permissible to pray upon its roof. He said — may peace be upon him — that whoever wishes to depart from Makkah should make his final act a circumambulation of the House, performing seven rounds, then bidding it farewell and placing his hand between the Black Stone and the Door, saying: "O Allah, I am Your servant and the son of Your servant and the son of Your handmaid. You carried me upon Your conveyance and guided me through Your land until You caused me to enter Your sanctuary. O Allah, this is Your House, and it has been —"


<!-- page 183 -->
I have examined it and hoped, in my sincere faith, that You would forgive me my sins — so if You have forgiven me, increase my contentment and keep my heart with You; and if You have not yet forgiven me, then forgive me now, before I depart from Your House. This is the hour of my departure — whether You grant me leave willingly or otherwise. I am leaving Your House, yet I shall exchange neither You nor Your House for anything else. Preserve me on my right and on my left, behind me and before me, until You return me safely to my family; and if You take me unto Yourself, do not abandon me — let me die as Your righteous servants, the foremost of Your creation, have died.

Then turn to face the House and say: O God, do not make this my final covenant with Your House. Go out and stand at the door of the mosque, turn your face toward the House, fill your eyes with the sight of it, raise your hands toward the sky, and say: O God, return me safe, successful, and forgiven. O God, if You cause me to die, forgive me; and if You grant me life, grant me a Hajj that is accepted. Do not make this my last covenant with Your Sacred House.

He said, peace be upon him, that it is recommended for one who intends to depart from Mecca to purchase dates for dirhams, or whatever he is able, and give them in charity, intending thereby to purify all that remains with him from the violations and excesses of his state of consecration. He said, peace be upon him, that one departing from Hajj should not feel as though he is turning away from what he dislikes toward what he loves; rather, let him depart with tranquility in his heart.

Ali, peace be upon him, did not permit testimony from a returning pilgrim. One day he went out to al-Rahbah and saw a gathering of people. He asked what the occasion was, and was told it was a group of returning pilgrims. He said: No greeting and no welcome — you have lightened your prayers and burdened your noon rest.

**Mentioning the Individual 'Umrah**

Allah the Almighty said: *"And complete Hajj and 'Umrah for Allah"* [Al-Baqarah: 196]. The Ahl al-Bayt, peace be upon them, and those among the reciters who agreed with them, read *'Umrah* in the nominative case, giving it the same positive and complete meaning as Hajj.

<!-- page 184 -->
They said it is obligatory, while some of those who differed from them recited *'Umrah* in the accusative case, as though cutting it off from the preceding words — that is, as though it were not linked to the command addressed to Allah the Almighty. Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, said: 'Umrah is an obligatory duty, just as Hajj is, because Allah the Almighty said: *"Complete Hajj and 'Umrah."* It was also narrated from Ali, peace be upon him, that he said: 'Umrah is obligatory. It was narrated from al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, that he said: 'Umrah to 'Umrah is an expiation for what comes between them.

The Messenger of Allah, may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him and his family, said: 'Umrah in the month of Ramadan is equivalent to Hajj. For the individual 'Umrah, one enters the state of ihram from the miqat, avoids everything that one in ihram must avoid, intends the House, performs seven circuits of circumambulation, walks between Safa and Marwah, prays two rak'ahs of the circumambulation, and is then released from 'Umrah. If one has brought a sacrificial animal to Mecca, al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: Perform 'Umrah in any month you wish. I prefer that 'Umrah be in the month of Rajab. If one performs 'Umrah in the month of Hajj and departs, it counts as a single 'Umrah. If one then performs Hajj in that same year, one is performing the Hajj al-Qiran.

**Mentioning Repulsion and Siege**

Allah the Almighty said: *"It is they who disbelieved and barred you from the Sacred Mosque, and the offering, prevented from reaching its place"* [Al-Fath: 25]. And He said, Blessed and Exalted: *"If you are prevented, then whatever offering is feasible"* [Al-Baqarah: 196]. Al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: Repulsion is prevention, and siege is illness. He said, peace be upon him, that the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, set out from Medina in the year of al-Hudaybiyyah with more than a thousand of his companions, intending 'Umrah. He entered ihram from Dhu al-Hulayfa, put the sacrificial animals in their marking collars, and directed them. Word of this reached the Quraysh — before the conquest of Mecca — and they rushed out in great numbers to bar his way.

<!-- page 185 -->
When they had reached 'Asfan, news came to him of this. He said: We have not come to fight anyone; we have come only to perform 'Umrah. If you wish, we will remain; let God judge between us, for He is the best of judges. The Quraysh sent their emissaries back and forth among the people, back and forth they went, and a long account followed — the details of which are well known — culminating in the fact that the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, called a truce with them for a period, by which he would forgo that year's visit, and return the following year for 'Umrah, along with whoever wished to accompany him. He then slaughtered the sacrificial animals he had brought at the spot where he stood, and he and the Muslims with him had their hair cut short and departed.

This is the ruling for one who is turned back from the House after having made Hajj or 'Umrah or both obligatory upon himself. If he has passed the miqat, he may be turned back before reaching the miqat — whether he had already entered ihram or not. If he had brought a sacrificial animal, he may slaughter it wherever he finds himself if he chooses and may do with it as he sees fit, since the obligation has not yet been firmly established. I have mentioned above that the obligation of the sacrificial gift is established by its being marked and collared, and that this is only after entering ihram from the miqat. Whoever enters ihram without being at the miqat is not in a valid state of ihram and has erred and violated the Sunnah.

The siege, however, is illness — as al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, explained in the words of Allah the Almighty. He was asked about a man who came with an illness and with a sacrificial animal he had sent ahead with his companions, set for a specific date: if he is in Hajj, the place for slaughtering the offering is on the Day of Sacrifice; if he is in 'Umrah, let him consider when his companions will arrive in Mecca and what hour they are preparing for the slaughter. If the hour is near, this also applies — that is, if he had entered ihram from the miqat and fell ill afterward. However, if he had not yet reached the miqat, he may depart if he wishes or remain, because the obligation of Hajj had not yet become binding upon him.

It was narrated that al-Husayn ibn Ali, peace be upon him, set out from Medina intending to perform 'Umrah, but when he fell ill along the way, news of his condition reached Ali, peace be upon him, while he was still in the city. Ali followed him and said: My son, do not complain. He said: I am suffering from pain in my head. So he, peace be upon him, slaughtered the offering on his behalf.

<!-- page 186 -->
He cut his hair and shaved his head and sent him back to Medina. When he recovered, he went out and performed 'Umrah.

Likewise, one who is confined by illness after entering ihram for Hajj, and who intends to perform 'Umrah or Hajj or both, is obliged to slaughter blood, as Allah the Almighty said: *"If you are prevented, then whatever offering is feasible"* [Al-Baqarah: 196]. This is the minimum — a sheep or more — and he returns. When he recovers from his illness, he goes out and makes up what he had been prevented from completing, whether it was Hajj, 'Umrah, or both.

One who is besieged by illness does not approach women, even within what is otherwise permitted to him. One who is merely turned back may approach women and has no obligation to make up what he missed, and does not owe any penalty for it. This is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, peace be upon them. Al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, was asked about one who was besieged by illness and then recovered — whether women were then lawful for him. He said: No, not until he has circumambulated the House. He was asked: What then of the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, when he returned from al-Hudaybiyyah and had not circumambulated the House? He said: He was turned back, not besieged — and for the one who is besieged, it is not permissible until he circumambulates.

**Mentioning Hajj on Behalf of the Incapacitated and the Deceased**

A woman from the tribe of Khath'am came to the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, and said: O Messenger of Allah, my father is a very old man, frail and weak, who has reached the age at which the obligation of Hajj applies but is unable to perform it. He said: Yes, perform it on his behalf — for if your father had a debt, you would discharge it on his account, and he would be rewarded for that accordingly. Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, was asked about a similar matter, and he permitted it. He cited the account of the Khath'ami woman, and mentioned the case of the two elderly men who had despaired of being able to perform Hajj — each of them is rewarded if he equips someone to perform Hajj on his behalf. However, one who is able to make the journey is not rewarded by equipping another; he must go out himself and perform Hajj in person, whether by camel or otherwise, even if he fears an enemy — because in the case of one who hopes that the fear will pass and the road to Hajj will open, there is no fixed time for Hajj.

<!-- page 187 -->
It is a once-in-a-lifetime obligation, and as long as one hopes to be able to perform it, the obligation remains upon him. If he ultimately finds a way, yet delays, he has wronged himself. Hajj is performed on behalf of the deceased if the deceased bequeathed it, or if his guardian volunteers to equip someone to perform it on his behalf. If the deceased bequeathed that Hajj be performed on his behalf from one-third of his estate, it is taken from that third. If he did not specify the third and he was a person of means, Hajj is taken from the totality of his estate — it being a bequest he made, subsequent to his other bequests, from the third.

Whoever is rewarded for performing Hajj on behalf of another, let him prefer that reward over his own expenses, for the Hajj is credited both to him and to the one on whose behalf he performs it. Whoever has not yet performed Hajj for himself performs it for a wage. If he has already performed Hajj for himself, then it is better. A man may perform Hajj on behalf of a man, and a woman may perform Hajj on behalf of a woman. If a woman performs Hajj on behalf of a man, it is permissible for him if he cannot find another, or if she is better than those he can find among men and can perform the rites fully. The man who performs Hajj on behalf of another should strive diligently to complete the Hajj and fulfil all its rites. It was narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, that after his son Ismail, peace be upon him, died, a man was hired to perform Hajj on his behalf. He instructed him in what to do during Hajj and stipulated conditions upon him. He then said to him: I know that if you fulfil what we have stipulated of you, it will be a valid argument for the one on whose behalf you have fulfilled the condition — and you will be rewarded for your bodily effort.

The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him and his family, said that Allah the Almighty will admit into Paradise three who are recommended to perform Hajj and whose executor carries out the bequest after their death: the pilgrim who strives and fulfils his duty. Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: It is permissible for one who performs Hajj on behalf of another for a wage to add, on his own behalf as a volunteer, whatever additional circumambulations or 'Umrah acts he wishes. He also said, may Allah's prayers be upon him: Whoever performs Hajj on behalf of another, let him say in his intention: O God, I intend to perform Hajj on behalf of so-and-so — accept it from him and reward me for my undertaking on his behalf. He should hold this intention at every stage and scene, in every act of Hajj — whether in poetry or in deed.

<!-- page 188 -->
**Mentioning Missed Hajj and 'Umrah**

Whoever is performing the Hajj al-Tamattu' — enjoying the interval between 'Umrah and Hajj — or the Hajj al-Qiran, and does not reach Mecca except at a time when he fears he will miss the standing, should complete his 'Umrah. If he has missed Hajj in that first instance, he follows the situation through to complete a single Hajj and resumes 'Umrah after that.

One who recognizes that the people have departed and who reaches Arafat before sunset has attained Hajj. If he does not settle at Mina, Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: if he reaches Arafat after the people have overflowed from it, stands there during the night, and remembers Allah therein, then joins the crowd before the people overflow from Muzdalifah, he has attained Hajj. If he does not reach Muzdalifah until after the people have overflowed from it and the sun has already risen, then Hajj has been missed.

If one misses Hajj, let him convert his ihram to 'Umrah, and the Hajj remains upon him — for whoever meets the occasion — unless he stipulated at the time of entering ihram that his release from ihram comes whenever he is prevented, in which case he does not owe Hajj for the missed occasion. He must, however, perform Hajj if he is able and can find the way. If he does not attain it until after the days of Hajj have passed, he should circumambulate the House and make it an individual 'Umrah, whether he is in Hajj al-Qiran or Hajj al-Tamattu'. If he entered ihram for Hajj alone, he does not owe 'Umrah, because he did not intend 'Umrah; rather, he must return to the miqat and enter ihram from there.

**Mentioning the Vow of Hajj**

Allah the Almighty said: *"A fountain from which the servants of Allah drink, causing it to gush forth abundantly — they fulfil their vows and fear a Day whose evil is widespread"* [Al-Insan: 6–7]. We will discuss in the chapters on vows what is obligatory and what is not obligatory, God willing. Whoever vows to perform Hajj must fulfil that vow, and it counts as rewarded Hajj on his behalf from the Hajj of Islam. Abu 'Abdillah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him, said: If one intends to do so and pronounces it, it will be rewarded for him.


<!-- page 189 -->
If he performs Hajj on behalf of others and has vowed to perform Hajj, that will fulfill his vow and count on behalf of his prayers to Allah. When it was said, "Whoever vows to perform Hajj but is unable to do so, let him send his representative, and abstaining will be required of him until his proxy fulfills it on his behalf." Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: "Whoever makes a vow that it is obligatory upon him to walk to the Sacred House of God, let him walk even if he grows weary and finds himself unable to walk on his knees." He gave of himself as much as he was able, for God does not burden a soul beyond its capacity. If a person reaches the limit of his capacity, there is nothing more required of him. As for the vow, the obligation applies only to what involves obedience; whoever vows to commit a sin has nothing binding upon him.

**On the gates of jihad — the obligation of jihad and the virtues therein:**

Allah Almighty said: "Fighting is ordained for you, though it is hateful to you. It may be that you dislike something that is good for you, and it may be that you love something that is bad for you." [Al-Baqarah: 216] He said: "When the sacred months have passed, slay the polytheists wherever you find them." [Al-Tawbah: 5] He said: "Permission is granted to those who are fought against because they have been wronged, and Allah is indeed able to grant them victory — those who were driven from their homes unjustly, only because they said, 'Allah is our Lord.'" [Al-Hajj: 39–40] Al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him, said regarding whatever was in the hands of the people of disbelief and misguidance: the believers overcame it and took it back from the hands of the polytheists. The return is attributed to the faithful. If they do so, then Allah is Forgiving, Merciful. [Al-Baqarah: 226] — that is, they returned to their husbands. Likewise, whatever the people of truth had lost of what was in their hands

<!-- page 190 -->
from the people of falsehood now belonged to what Allah had fulfilled for them — meaning His response to them. From this comes the saying of the Almighty: "returned to His Messenger" [Al-Hashr: 7] — that is, returned to him. And this is the meaning of Allah's words: "Permission is granted to those who are fought against because they have been wronged" [Al-Hajj: 39] — meaning the people of truth, while those who wrong them are the people of falsehood.

By defeating the latter despite what Allah Almighty had granted to the former, Allah Almighty gave the people of truth permission to fight those who had oppressed them and taken what He had made theirs. He enjoined upon the Muslims the obligation of waging jihad against the polytheists among those who came to them with the Scripture, so that they would either submit or pay the jizyah while in a state of subjection. Most of those who say "Fight those who do not believe in Allah or the Last Day, who do not forbid what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden, and who do not follow the religion of truth from among those who were given the Book, until they pay the jizyah from their hand while they are subdued" [Al-Tawbah: 29] — Allah Almighty prescribed this for them. And it was said: the polytheists who are not People of the Book must submit, and most said: "And fight the polytheists all together as they fight you all together." [Al-Tawbah: 36] He said: "When the sacred months have passed, kill the polytheists wherever you find them, and capture them, besiege them, and sit in wait for them at every place of ambush. But if they repent, establish prayer, and give zakah, then let them go their way." [Al-Tawbah: 5]

From the polytheists who are not People of the Book, only Islam or the sword was accepted. From the People of the Book, the jizyah was accepted on the condition that they submit to the rule of Islam, until Allah Almighty guides them or they perish upon error. The argument had already been established against them. Allah Almighty urged, in His Book and upon the tongue of

<!-- page 191 -->
His Messenger, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, the undertaking of jihad for His sake, commanded it and praised it. He said, Almighty: "Go forth, light and heavy, and strive with your wealth and yourselves in the way of Allah." [Al-Tawbah: 41] Al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him, said regarding the words of Allah the Almighty: "Go forth, light and heavy" — he said: "I mean the young and the old." May Allah bless and exalt him.

Allah said: "Indeed, Allah has purchased from the believers their lives and their wealth, in exchange for Paradise. They fight in the way of Allah, so they slay and are slain — a promise binding upon Him in the Torah, the Gospel, and the Qur'an. And who is more faithful to his covenant than Allah? So rejoice in the sale you have made with Him, and that is the great triumph." [Al-Tawbah: 111] Abu Abdullah Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, was asked about this verse. It was said to him: "Is whoever strives for the sake of Allah among the people of this verse?" He said, peace be upon him: "No — for Allah Almighty said that He purchased from the believers their lives and their wealth, promising them Paradise, and then He promised them further and described those who come after this verse with specific qualities." He said: "The repentant, the worshippers, the praisers, the wanderers, the bowing, the prostrating, those who enjoin what is good and forbid what is evil, and those who observe the limits of Allah — so give glad tidings to the believers." [Al-Tawbah: 112] Whoever possesses these qualities is among those to whom Allah has given His promise. As for one who does not possess these qualities but strives seeking the gains of this world or its glory, or is coerced into jihad while not being a keeper of his religion or among the people of this characteristic that Allah Almighty described in His Book — he was among those about whom it was said: "May Allah support this religion through people who have no share in it." And the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, said: "The mujahideen for the sake of Allah are the leaders of the people of Paradise." And the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

<!-- page 192 -->
"Save what was for the sake of Allah" — and the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said: "Above all righteousness, until a man is killed for the sake of Allah, meaning there is no righteousness above that." And the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said: "There is no drop more beloved to Allah than a drop of blood shed for the sake of Allah, or a drop of tear shed from fear of Allah." He said, peace be upon him, that Allah and His angels send prayers upon the owners of horses who kept and prepared them. The Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said: "Spending on horses for the sake of Allah is like the spending of which Allah Almighty said: 'Those who spend their wealth by night and by day, secretly and publicly.'" [Al-Baqarah: 274] He said: "Strive in the way of Allah with your own hands; if you are unable, then strive with your tongues; and if you are unable, then strive with your hearts." He said, peace be upon him: "Jihad for the sake of Allah is one of the gates of Paradise." And he said, peace be upon him, regarding the words of Allah Almighty — "And the garment of taqwa is better" [Al-A'raf: 26] — he said: "It is the garment of weapons for the sake of Allah."

He said: Jihad for the sake of Allah is obligatory upon all Muslims, according to Allah Almighty's words: "Fighting is ordained for you." If a group of Muslims fulfills the jihad sufficiently, that obligation is lifted from those who remain behind. However, if they are needed, then all Muslims are obligated to go forth and support them until they are satisfied. He recited the words of Allah Almighty: "And it was not for the believers to go forth all at once." [Al-Tawbah: 122] — meaning that if a group of them performs jihad, the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him and his family, said: the Arabs among the Muslims have no obligation of jihad except when they are needed, and there is no share for one who does not strive in what those who do strive obtain. The peoples of the Arab lands today — such as the people of the fertile regions, the valleys, and the cities — those who do not fight well and have no desire to fight: the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, gave permission

<!-- page 193 -->
to the one who is cowardly in battle to refrain from fighting, saying that a coward should not participate in a raid lest he be routed quickly. Rather, let him see what he intended to bring to the raid and prepare others with it, for he will have the like of their reward, and nothing of their reward will be diminished — because those we mentioned abstain from fighting and are not among its people, while being in the situation of the Arabs. If they have wealth, they must spend it for the sake of Allah, because Allah Almighty has imposed jihad upon Muslims with their wealth as well. Those who are unable to fight in person should strive with their wealth, as the Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said about the coward.

If enemies attack the Muslims — and we seek refuge in Allah, for this is a most grave matter — then it is the duty of all people to go forth for the sake of Allah. In this regard, Allah the Almighty says: "Go forth, light and heavy" [Al-Tawbah: 41] and He says: "And fight" [Al-Tawbah: 36] — then the common people and their servants, their elders and their youth alike, must all go forth until Allah Almighty repels the enemy from them. There is no obligation of jihad upon slaves in circumstances other than this, nor upon women, nor upon boys. However, if a woman wishes to go out with her husband or with a mahram to tend to the sick and wounded and to provide for the mujahideen as much as she is able, there is nothing wrong with that — even if she has a husband, she may not go out except with his permission; likewise, a slave may not go out except with the permission of his master.

**On supplication, righteous deeds, and the etiquette of travel in jihad:**

Allah Almighty said: "And when they went forth against Goliath and his soldiers, they said: 'Our Lord, pour upon us patience, and plant firmly our feet, and give us victory over the disbelieving people.'" [Al-Baqarah: 250] And Allah said: "The Messenger has believed in what was revealed to him from his Lord, and so have the believers." [Al-Baqarah: 285–286] The Messenger of Allah, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

<!-- page 194 -->
"You are our Master, so grant us victory over the disbelieving people."

Peace and blessings of Allah be upon him: a man who wishes to travel and leave his family should appoint a successor better than two rak'ahs he prays when he departs, then say: "O Allah, I entrust to You myself, my family, my wealth, my religion, my worldly affairs, my hereafter, my trust, and the conclusion of my deeds." No believer does this except that Allah grants him what he asks.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad, may Allah's prayers be upon him, said: "When you wish to travel, pray two rak'ahs and then say: 'O Allah, protect me and preserve what I have entrusted to You, and inform me and tell me the good of what I have with You.' Then ask Him for your needs — what you seek from your travels, and for your family and others."

Abu Ja'far ibn Muhammad ibn Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, — who was Abu Ali ibn al-Husayn, peace be upon him — when he wished to travel or set out with some of his wealth, would pray two rak'ahs and make supplication at length. When he was ready to depart, he would place his foot in the stirrup and purchase his safety from Allah as much as he could — meaning he would give in charity when departing. And when Allah returned him safely, he would praise Allah and thank Him, and likewise give charity as much as was possible.

A commander of the believers, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him, set out on one of his journeys, and when he was settled upon his mount he said: "Praise be to Allah who has blessed us with His innumerable blessings. May Allah bless Muhammad, His Prophet, and his family. Glory be to the One who has subjected this to us, and we were not capable of it ourselves, and indeed to our Lord we shall return." This is taken from the Book of Allah Almighty — it is mentioned in the words of the Almighty as the verse established upon it. He said, peace and blessings of Allah be upon him: "I bear witness that there is no god but Allah alone, and I bear witness that Muhammad is His slave and His messenger. O Allah, I seek refuge in You from the hardships of travel, the grief of returning, and the ill sight of parents, wealth, and children. O Allah, You are the Companion in travel, the Successor over the family, and the Helper in all affairs. Lengthen our journey for us, and ease the grief from us, and grant us sufficiency in all that matters, for You are capable of all things."

He, peace be upon him, said: "Among the ways of travelling, when people set out, they should gather together and accompany one another on the journey, pooling their provisions and spending together — that is better for them, better for one another, and better for themselves." Al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn said:


<!-- page 195 -->
The Prophet Muhammad, peace be upon him, spoke of proper conduct — muruwwa — both in settled life and during travel. In settled life, this means reciting the Qur'an, attending mosques, keeping the company of righteous people, and deepening one's understanding of jurisprudence. When travelling, it means spending generously of one's surplus provisions, avoiding disputes with one's companions, and speaking well of them if the group disperses. He also forbade allowing riding animals to perish through neglect, and forbade loading them beyond their capacity or using them as mere resting platforms.

He said that the owner of a riding animal bears greater responsibility than the rider. He further enumerated six rights that an animal has over its owner: that its owner begins by feeding it when he dismounts; that he waters it when water is at hand; that he does not strike it except in what is due; that he does not burden it with more than it can carry; and that he does not force it to walk distances beyond its strength, nor press it onward when it has no more to give.

Regarding the recitation of Qur'anic verses, it is related that one day the Commander of the Believers said that Muhammad had been sent with the truth and honored his household through it, and that nothing afflicted by the Qur'an would remain harmed. He invited those present to put their questions to him, and a man rose and said, "O Commander of the Believers, my mare has become unruly toward me, and I am bewildered by it." He instructed him to recite into her right ear the verse: *"And to Him submits whoever is in the heavens and the earth, willingly or unwillingly, and to Him they will be returned"* [Āl 'Imrān: 83]. The man did so, and his horse became tractable.

When the people crowded the road with the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, during some of his expeditions, he ordered a caller to announce that narrowness of the road did not constitute grounds for abandoning jihad. He also said that the owner of a riding animal has the greater right to the path over a man on foot, and that a man on foot has the greater right over one who has removed his sandals. He said further that Allah loves gentleness and aids those who show it. If you ride these animals — meaning the beasts of burden — when the land is hard and unyielding, dismount and let them recover, moving along at a measured pace. If the earth is fertile, give the animals their due rest. And you must travel by night, for the earth is traversed more readily in darkness than in daylight. Do not camp at the edges of roads, for they are the paths of wild creatures and the shelter of

<!-- page 196 -->
serpents. The Prophet, peace be upon him, instructed the infantry to walk at a steady pace when they were marching on foot, saying that this would spare them from exhaustion.

The Prophet, peace be upon him, also said that his community would be kept safe from drowning if, when they embarked upon a vessel, they recited: *"In the name of Allah, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful — they did not esteem Allah as He deserves to be esteemed, yet the whole earth is in His grip on the Day of Resurrection, and the heavens are folded in His right hand; exalted is He above what they associate with Him"* [al-Zumar: 67], and then: *"In the name of Allah is its course and its anchorage; indeed, my Lord is Forgiving and Merciful."*

From 'Alī, peace be upon him, it is transmitted that he added to that supplication: "O Allah, bless us in our vessel, make our journey excellent, and preserve us from the evil of our sea."

**On what the commanders of armies are obliged to observe toward those under their command:**

Allah the Almighty said: *"O you who believe, obey Allah and obey the Messenger and those in authority among you"* [al-Nisā': 59]. The Imams, peace be upon them, bear the whole of this command. Whoever holds authority over a matter is the guardian of that matter, and it is obligatory to obey him in what is good. If he commands something evil or issues an order that openly contradicts what is right, obedience in that does not apply.

It was narrated that the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, dispatched a man in command over a group from the Ansar and instructed them to obey him. The commander became angered over something and said, "Gather wood for me." They did so. He then ordered them to gather more until a great pile had been assembled, and he ordered it to be set alight. When the fire was blazing, he said, "Did not the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, command you to obey me?" They said, "Yes." He then ordered them to enter the fire. Some among them were inclined toward obedience and moved toward it; others held them back, saying, "We fled to the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, precisely to escape the fire." They remained in this state until the fire died down and the commander's anger subsided.

When the man reached the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, and reported what had happened, he said: "Had they entered it, they would not have come out

<!-- page 197 -->
of it until the Day of Resurrection. Obedience is only in what is good." Thus, when an Imam appoints a commander or assigns a man as a worker, that man is to obey him and carry out his orders, completing what he is charged with and not preceding the commander in any matter. Obeying that commander is obedience to Allah, obedience to the Messenger of Allah, and obedience to the Imam who appointed him.

If those under command come to see that he has exceeded what he was charged with, they should refer the matter to the Imam. Once they have reported it to him, their responsibility is discharged and the matter rests with the Imam, peace be upon him, who must examine it according to what Allah the Almighty has disclosed. If it becomes apparent that a man has broken from obedience to the Imam and is in open disagreement with him, his claim to obedience from the others ceases, and those appointed by the Imam must fight against him alongside those who call to a just leader.

Whoever is appointed by a just Imam to a task must respond to that appointment by taking on what he is capable of and able to perform, and must not work under or be appointed by unjust rulers or the partisans of prejudice.

It has been narrated from Ja'far ibn Muhammad, peace be upon him, that he said: "To hold guardianship under just rulers, to work with them, to assist them in their governance, and to earn lawful income through them — all of this is permitted. But to hold guardianship under corrupt and unjust rulers, to serve as their agent and support their authority, and to earn income through them — part of this is forbidden." The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, forbade assisting wrongdoers, and it was said: "The oppressor, the one appointed to assist him, and the one who is content with him are partners in sin."

**On what the princes are obliged to observe:**

Allah the Almighty said: *"Indeed, Allah enjoins justice and charity"* [al-Nahl: 90], and He said: *"Indeed, Allah commands you to restore trusts to their rightful owners, and when you judge between people, to judge with justice"* [al-Nisā': 58].

The covenant of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, to some of the commanders of his armies was a covenant in which he directed them to let their hearts be filled with mercy

<!-- page 198 -->
for those entrusted to them, compassion toward them, and goodwill in their dealings with them. He instructed them not to be harsh in seizing upon their people's slips and mistakes, for these men were their brothers by lineage and their equals in creation — prone to error and subject to afflictions, with their hands entrusted to carry out the work. He counselled the commanders to extend forgiveness and pardon as their people deserved, in the measure that they themselves would wish forgiveness and pardon to be extended to them. He urged them to deal justly with themselves and with their own households just as they dealt justly with others, and not to expose the faults and private failings of their people when those were concealed from them — indeed, those most hateful in their sight should be those who brought such failings to light. He instructed them to keep close to the people of piety and integrity, and to be wary of those who flatter excessively, for excessive praise breeds arrogance. He told them to know the people of virtue by their goodness and to distinguish those who transgress proper limits. Every person was to be honored or corrected in proportion to what his condition deserved. He commanded them to judge with justice, for just judgment is a balance that Allah has placed upon the earth and established on its proper course, so that each person receives what is rightfully theirs.

He further instructed them to uphold their covenant, to honor their conscience, and to be cautious about shedding blood without verification and clear evidence. He said that no one is fit to enjoin good and forbid evil unless he possesses three qualities: knowledge of what he enjoins, knowledge of what he forbids, and justice in what he commands and what he prohibits.

**On the call to Islam before fighting:**

Allah the Almighty said: *"Invite to the way of your Lord with wisdom and good counsel"* [al-Nahl: 125]. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, used to send an army or a detachment with counsel to their commander to maintain God-consciousness within himself and to deal well with the Muslims under his command. He would say: "Invade in the name of Allah and for the sake of Allah and upon the religion of the Messenger of Allah. Do not fight a people until you have presented the call to them. Invite them

<!-- page 199 -->
to bear witness that there is no god but Allah and that Muhammad is the messenger of Allah, and to acknowledge what I have brought from Allah. If they answer you, they are your brothers in religion. Then invite them to move from their land to the land of the emigrants. If they do so, well and good; if they decline, inform them that they shall be like the Arabs of the Muslims, subject to the judgment of Allah that governs all Muslims, but they shall have no share in the spoils or the booty. If they have already embraced Islam, call them to pay the jizya from willing hands while they are under your power. If they answer, accept it from them and leave them in peace. If they refuse, then seek the help of Allah against them and fight them. Do not kill a child, an aged man, or a woman — that is, those who do not fight you. Do not act treacherously, and do not violate pledges." This is what the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, commanded: that the polytheists be called to Islam before fighting.

Whoever has been invited in this manner, and for those with whom relations have deteriorated, the call should still be made — to come to the truth and enter into obedience before the sword is raised, to submit with wisdom and sound counsel. If, however, they already know what they are being called to and what is being fought over, then if they initiate fighting before the call is made, there is nothing wrong with responding in kind. The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, was engaged in a night raid against the Banū al-Mustaliq; he attacked them before making a formal call, and it was said that he killed their fighters and took their women and children captive, having come upon them while they were unaware. This was because the call had already reached them in their own tongue. It is therefore permissible to fight before repeating the call when people already know what they are being called to.

**On what is recommended in combat and before it:**

It is established that the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, sent a man from Khuzā'ah as a scout during the year of al-Hudaybiyah to gather intelligence about the Quraysh and bring him news of them. He also used to dispatch advance parties ahead of his armies to uncover watering points and gather intelligence about the enemy. He, peace be upon him and his family, ordered that a trench be dug around his army's encampment. He used to instruct his men to adopt a watchword

<!-- page 200 -->
by which they could recognize one another during fighting and close combat. He used to urge them toward patience, reminding them of the weighty reward awaiting them, warning them of the shame of flight, and encouraging steadfastness.

When the enemy was met, he, peace be upon him and his family, would say: "O Allah, You are my fortress and my support and my strength. O Allah, with You I fight." On another occasion he said: "O Allah, praise is Yours; to You is the complaint, and You are the helper." And Jibrīl, peace be upon him, came down to him and said, "O Muhammad, you have called upon Allah by His greatest name."

He also said, peace be upon him: "Seize the moment of supplication at five times — when the Qur'an is being recited, at the call to prayer, when the rain descends, when the two ranks meet in battle, and when the wronged person supplicates — for it is not veiled from the Throne."

The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, used to arrange the men, the horses, the camels, and the baggage behind them to project greater numbers. He also organized them by tribe, making each group a distinct unit on one side of the formation, each with its own banner, so that anyone separated from his brigade could look for it and rejoin it.

He did not like to begin fighting until the sun had moved past its zenith and the wind was blowing favorably, and Allah's prayers are prominent for those who stand out. He said, peace be upon him, recounting the day he was sent out at Khaybar: "The Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, said to me as I went out to engage in single combat: 'O Allah, support me and do not abandon me; O Allah, grant me victory or grant me what You will; O Allah, take charge of me and do not leave me unguarded; O Allah, make me one who remembers You, is grateful to You, faithful to You, and obedient to You.' I killed my adversary that day and left the spoils, choosing between killing and taking." 

The supplication of the Messenger of Allah, peace be upon him, on the day of al-Ahzāb was: "O You who answers the cry of the distressed, O You who answers the call of those in need — relieve my anguish. You know my condition and the condition of my companions. Protect me from the terrors of my enemy." And when he put on his armor to fight, he, peace be upon him, would say: "O Allah, You are my fortress." He also said, peace be upon him, that when the Muslims stand facing their enemy, they should stand firm upon their positions.


<!-- page 201 -->

Each fighter must hold his position. If he sees an opening created by the enemy, he may exploit it and then return to his place. When the enemy initiates the campaign, they must hold their positions and stand firm, for Allah Almighty says: "O you who believe, if you meet a group, then stand firm and remember Allah much, so that you may succeed." [Al-Anfal: 45] He also said: "Allah loves those who fight in His cause in a row as if they were a solid structure." And it was said: "If the enemy is defeated, they should not be pursued immediately — not until the threat of a counterattack has passed and security is established. Then all forces should press forward and intensify the killing." I seek refuge in Allah from defeat among the Muslims. If such a defeat occurs, the commander and the Imam — if present — must hold firm, and the people will be rallied back. They should remind one another of what Allah has promised to those who remain steadfast, and call to mind the example of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), who stood firm on the Day of Uhud when most of the people had abandoned him.
