It is narrated: "I remained with the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) on that rocky ground, shielding him with myself — defending my hand once and my face once — while the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) was wounded and many of the people had fled toward the city, some even saying that the Messenger of Allah had been killed. Yet he remained firm until Allah Almighty defeated the polytheists from him." It was also said: it is permissible for a lone fighter to hold his position against an enemy battalion if he trusts in himself, and it is permissible for him to advance with the spear, thrusting at the enemy, so that his companions may rise up around him with their swords or their spears — whatever weapons they carry — though this should not be done carelessly. He then cited the words of Allah Almighty: "So do not take flight from them. Whoever turns his back to them on that day — unless maneuvering for battle or rallying to a group — has incurred the wrath of Allah, and his refuge is Hell, and what a wretched destination." [Al-Anfal: 15] He said: "Fleeing from battle is among the gravest of sins." The prohibition on flight applies when one is attempting to stand against the enemy and return to one's ranks. However, it has been mentioned regarding

<!-- page 202 -->

flight and defeat that Allah Almighty had obligated Muslims to stand firm against ten times their number. He said: "If there are twenty of you who are patient, they will overcome two hundred; and if there are a hundred of you, they will overcome a thousand." [Al-Anfal: 65] This was a heavy burden upon the Muslims, so Allah lightened it for them. He said: "Now Allah has lightened your burden, for He knows that you are weak. If there are a hundred of you who are patient, they will overcome two hundred; and if there are a thousand of you, they will overcome two thousand." [Al-Anfal: 66] Accordingly, if a man flees from two enemies among the polytheists or a group equal to his own numbers or fewer, he has entered into the ruling of the prohibition and committed a grave sin. However, if they flee from a force greater than that after having exhausted their efforts and expended all their energy, there is nothing against them. If they withdraw from fighting, they should occupy themselves with fatigue relief and preparation — patrolling to guard against the enemy's reserves and stratagems. If the enemy retreats to their fortresses or their trenches, the Muslims do not return until they have reached their full exertion; when their strength is restored and their energy renewed, they press upon them. If the enemy is besieged, the Muslims fight them.

**On the killing of polytheists:**

Allah Almighty said: "Kill the polytheists wherever you find them, and capture them, and besiege them, and sit in wait for them." [Al-Tawbah: 5] He also said: "And kill them wherever you encounter them and drive them out from where they drove you out." [Al-Baqarah: 191] It has been narrated from the Imams, may Allah's prayers be upon them, that they permitted the killing of polytheists by weapons, fire, stones, water, and anything else by which they could be killed. Catapults and armored siege engines were to be deployed against them if they were fortified in strongholds.

<!-- page 203 -->

If there are Muslims and dhimmis among them, along with those who should not be killed — such as children, women, the elderly, and those incapacitated who do not fight — one should not aim deliberately at them. Care must be taken, for one who deliberately kills a child, a woman, an elderly person, or an incapacitated person among the polytheists or monks has sinned. As for those who are killed from among these categories while Muslims are engaged against the polytheists — without deliberate intention — there is nothing upon the Muslim for that. Whoever of the polytheists had done good to the Muslims should not be killed. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said to the Muslims on the Day of Badr: "Whoever you are able to capture from the Bani Abd al-Muttalib, do not kill him, for they went out against their will."

Similarly, whoever among the polytheists displays a sign of Islam should not be killed. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) once dispatched an army toward the Khath'am tribe, and when the enemy became aware of the force, some of them prostrated in submission, yet some were killed while in prostration. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) condemned this and ordered that half of the blood money be paid. He then said: "I am free of every Muslim who takes up residence among polytheists." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) forbade the killing of messengers. If it becomes known that someone who has entered the abode of Islam is a polytheist messenger — without any covenant of safe conduct — he should not be killed, even if he claims to be a messenger but brings no proof and his mission cannot be verified. In such a case, the Imam has a choice: if he wishes, he may kill him; if he wishes, he may take him captive; and if he wishes, he may release him. It is permissible to imprison the messenger if there is concern about his movements and fear about releasing him — unless the Imam finds reassurance on that score, in which case he exercises his judgment in the matter.

**On captives and those granted security:**

Allah Almighty said: "When you meet those who disbelieve, strike the necks until, when you have subdued them, bind the bonds firmly. Then either show favour or ransom until the war lays down its burdens."

<!-- page 204 -->

The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) captured a group from Quraysh on the Day of Badr, taking ransom from some and killing others. The Imam likewise has a choice regarding prisoners: if he wishes, he may kill them; if he wishes, he may release them; if he wishes, he may exchange them for Muslim captives; and if he wishes, he may take ransom from either the polytheists or from the captives themselves if they have wealth in the abode of war — whatever is found in their possession is subject to consideration. He must examine each case carefully and determine what is most pleasing to Allah Almighty.

Among those captured on the Day of Badr was al-Abbas, the uncle of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him). The Prophet knew his true intention — that he had been brought out with a group of Bani Abd al-Muttalib against his will. After al-Abbas was captured, he said: "O Prophet of Allah, I was already a Muslim." The Prophet replied: "I know, but your outward conduct was against us. You must ransom yourself." He was placed in the lot with the group of captives. When word reached the Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) that the bonds were causing al-Abbas pain, the Messenger groaned out of compassion for him, though he could only act as was proper. Al-Abbas was held captive at that time, and the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) replaced him — placing him in more comfortable conditions — and said to him: "I love you for your kinship, your standing, and for the love my father bore you." He passed by al-Abbas, who was in captivity, and turned away from him. He called: "O Aqeel, my brother — why is this rift between you and me? You see me yet give no answer." He reported this to the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), and they went to Aqeel. When Aqeel saw the Prophet, he said: "O Messenger of Allah, do not spare Abu Jahl — and if you are not going to kill him, at least intercede regarding the defeat." The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) smiled and said: "Allah has already killed him." He then said to al-Abbas: "Aqeel is with you — he has no money, but you are a man of wealth." Al-Abbas said: "O Messenger of Allah, I was struck down with gold ounces — count them against my ransom." The Prophet replied: "That is in the way of Allah — the mercy is for the Muslims. But send back what came to you from what you had left in Makkah." Al-Abbas said: "What money did I leave there?" The Prophet said: "Glory be to Allah — what of the money you entrusted to Umm al-Fadl before your departure, telling her to safeguard it until your return, and saying that if you were harmed, she should use it for such-and-such?"

<!-- page 205 -->

Al-Abbas stared at him and said: "I bear witness that you are truly the Messenger of Allah. By Allah, no one knew this except myself and her, and you could only have learned it from your Lord." He then sent for the wealth, ransomed himself with it, and ransomed Aqeel as well.

The Imam — and whoever acts on his behalf — must examine each captive from among the polytheists. If killing the fighters among them is of greater benefit to the Muslims, he should do so. If enslavement is better, he should take them captive and bind them. If granting them amnesty or accepting ransom is more beneficial, he does that — always acting upon whichever course Allah Almighty guides him to.

If the polytheists accept a guarantee of security and submit to the rule of the Muslims, or the rule of an arbitrator among them, the arbitrator may rule in whatever is permissible: by killing the fighters, by taking the offspring captive, by enslaving them, or by granting them the status of dhimmis if they refrain from Islam. Such a ruling is permissible and may be implemented. However, if he rules otherwise — for example, that they should be left alone without being raided, not killed, not required to pay the jizyah, or any ruling contrary to the Book of Allah Almighty and the Sunnah of His Prophet (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) — his ruling is reversed. They are not permitted to revert to their prior safety; rather, they are fought until they surrender or pay the jizyah.

They came down to the ruling of the commander of the armies, and it was not permissible to rule over them without their consent. If they submitted to the ruling of another party, no one may rule over them except the one to whom they submitted. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said regarding the Banu Qurayzah: "The ruling of Sa'd ibn Mu'adh is applicable." Sa'd ibn Mu'adh ruled that their fighters be killed, their offspring and women taken captive, and their wealth divided. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) permitted this ruling over them and said: "You have judged them by the judgment of Allah from above the seven heavens." If they submitted to the ruling of a man who then died before passing judgment, it seems appropriate that they accept another arbiter, or they revert to their prior state of safety and must be fought again.

The captives must be fed. If the captor intends to kill them, he must still feed them, for Allah Almighty says: "And they feed, out of love for Him, the poor, the orphan, and the captive." [Al-Insan: 8]

<!-- page 206 -->

As for Muslim captives held by the enemy, it is obligatory upon the Muslims to ransom them if they are able to do so. Al-Husayn ibn Ali, may Allah's prayers be upon him, was asked about a captive: who bears the obligation of ransoming him? He said: "The people of the land for which he was fighting." As for one who was captured through other circumstances, the obligation of ransoming him falls upon the Muslims in general. Whoever ransoms him freely as an act of charity and merit, it is obligatory to acknowledge that.

If Allah grants a group of Muslims safe passage through the polytheists on account of a guarantee of security, they descend under that trust. But the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Muslims are equal in their blood and they act on their conscience in matters below that." The commander of the armies must present to them the rule of Islam. If they accept it, they are not returned to their former state of safety, and their fighters are treated accordingly. Similarly, if any Muslim — whether a slave, a woman, or another — extends a guarantee of security, that guarantee is binding. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Try to be faithful to them in what is below that standard." If someone extended security to certain dhimmis, captives, a child, or a Muslim man in battle among the polytheists, their security should not be dishonoured. The sign of security is like its spoken declaration. It is valid in any tongue, provided it is understood and recognized.

If a polytheist enters the Muslim lands under safe conduct for trade, a tenth of the trade's value is taken from him for amounts exceeding two hundred dirhams or their equivalent. If he wishes to depart, he is not permitted to leave carrying weapons or anything that strengthens the polytheists in war — such as equipment that serves the house of Islam — and whatever he was prohibited from taking may not be returned to him. Even if a polytheist woman is entrusted with safe conduct and enters the abode of peace, her inviolability is severed from that of the polytheist man. If a believer embraces Islam in the abode of Islam, whatever wealth and children he left behind in the abode of shirk remain in the abode of polytheism — unless they appear to the Muslims. If he converted to Islam in the abode of polytheism and then entered the abode of peace as a Muslim, his children are Muslims by virtue of his Islam, and his wealth belongs to him. All of this is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them all.


<!-- page 207 -->

Mentioning Reconciliation, Conciliation, and Jizya:

Allah Almighty said: "Except those with whom you made a covenant among the polytheists, and they did not fail you in anything, and did not support anyone against you — so fulfill their covenant until their term. Surely Allah loves the pious." [al-Tawbah: 4] The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) made a covenant with the polytheists of Quraysh in the year of Hudaybiyah. The Imam, and whoever the Imam appoints, may consider making reconciliation between the Muslims and the polytheists if he sees benefit in it, bidding them farewell for a known period, and the most he may bid farewell to them is ten consecutive years. He fulfills the covenant with them as long as he sees good in it, but if after that he sees benefit for the Muslims in fighting them, he renounces the covenant, informs them that he intends to fight them, and grants them a respite before engaging them — time to warn their people — as the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) did when Allah Almighty revealed: "A declaration of immunity from Allah and His Messenger to those polytheists with whom you made a covenant." [al-Tawbah: 1] He sent 'Ali (peace be upon him) to recite it to them during the season and warn them. If the Imam had taken money from them for a covenant he wished to annul before its term, he returned to them what he had taken. If the polytheists were People of the Book, jizya was accepted from them and they were left in peace. However, jizya is not accepted from the Arab polytheists — they are to be fought until they surrender or are killed. If the dhimmis pay the jizya, nothing further may be taken from them beyond what was agreed, and a known, fixed tribute is levied on their lands for each year. Jizya is also taken from the Magi, who are regarded as People of an ancient Book. If a person from the people of jizya embraces Islam, the tribute placed upon him remains, and the tribute remains on his land. He is obligated to pay the tithe when the land produces, and if the land passes to a Muslim, it remains kharaj land, since the jizya was levied based on the persistence of the dhimmi people.

<!-- page 208 -->

The jizyah is not required from children, slaves, or women among the dhimma — only from adult free men. It is levied in three tiers each year: from every man of the upper class, those of means and capacity, forty-eight dirhams; from every man of the middle class, twenty-four dirhams; and from every man of the lower class, twelve dirhams. If a man among them is poor and unable to work, the jizya is lifted from him, for Allah Almighty says: "Allah does not burden a soul beyond its capacity." [al-Baqarah: 286] The kharaj on their lands and trees applies to all who hold land among them, whether great or small, man or woman. The jizya is not to be imposed on any of their elderly men unless there is benefit in it for the Muslims — in which case the Imam may impose it if he sees fit, or employ them to fight the polytheists. The tribute is also taken from the price of what they sell, even of what is not permissible for Muslims to sell, such as wine, pork, and the like, since most of their wealth comes from such things. It is permissible for Muslims to make pledges with them and take from them in jizya and in all that they owe. Muslims should not humiliate them, nor enter their churches uninvited, nor initiate greetings of peace with them; their hands are to be taken, and they are to be prevented from building new churches in the abode of Islam. However, they are permitted to maintain and repair what was already built or what has fallen into disrepair. They are to be barred from entering the sacred precincts. This is the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's blessings be upon them all.

Mentioning the Ruling on Spoils:

Allah Almighty said: "So eat of what you have taken as spoils — lawful and good." [al-Anfal: 69] And He said: "And know that whatever you take as spoils, a fifth belongs to Allah and the Messenger and to the near relatives, the orphans, the needy." [al-Anfal: 41] And He said: "And from the

<!-- page 209 -->

wealth that he earned, he will come bearing it on the Day of Resurrection, and then every soul will be recompensed for what it earned." The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) said regarding one who took a cloak from the booty without right: "I see it upon him in the Fire." The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) once returned from a raid, and 'Aqil ibn Abi Talib was with him. When he entered upon his wife, she said to him, "You have fought — did you bring us any of the booty?" He said, "No, by Allah — except for this needle, which I found and took." He pushed it to her, and she was surprised at what he said. Then they both heard a caller on behalf of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) announce: "O people, return the needle and the thread — do not misappropriate even a needle." 'Aqil said to his wife, "I see that your needle has missed its mark," and he took it from her and threw it back into the booty.

It is permissible to make use of the booty for the needs of jihad before it is formally sworn over — such as weapons, animals, and the like — but nothing of it is to be sold until the oath is taken. The commander of the army may choose for himself and take from it what he wishes. The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) sent 'Ali (peace be upon him) to Yemen and sent Khalid ibn al-Walid to another region, saying: "If you meet, let 'Ali be the commander over all of you; and if you separate, each of you leads his own mission." They met with their commander — may Allah's prayers be upon him — and encountered the enemy, defeated them, slew the fighters, and took captives. 'Ali (peace be upon him) chose from the booty a slave girl for himself. Khalid ibn al-Walid then wrote to the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) objecting to this. The Prophet replied: "Do not disparage 'Ali, for 'Ali is from me and I am from him, and he is your guardian after me." The Imam and the commander of the army may each take one thing for himself from the booty before it is distributed, if he wishes. If the Muslims are unable to carry the booty, they destroy it — they burn goods and weapons

<!-- page 210 -->

with fire, slaughter animals and livestock and burn them as well — rather than leave them to be used by the enemy or represent them as lost, nor do they leave behind any portion of the booty they cannot carry for the enemy to benefit from. As for wealth belonging to Muslims that has fallen into the hands of the polytheists, it belongs to its rightful owner and the polytheists cannot strip a Muslim of his property. The polytheists once raided the camels of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) and his family, and drove them away along with an old woman who was among the camels. The old woman rose in the night and recognized the camels of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him), and she came to Madinah with one of them. The people recognized it as the camel of the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) and brought the old woman to him. She told him what had happened and said, "O Messenger of Allah, I made a vow on my journey that if Allah granted me safe passage, I would slaughter this camel." The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) said to her: "That is not lawful — one may not fulfill a vow regarding something one does not own." He took back the camel (peace and blessings be upon him).

If the Imam or the commander of the army designates a reward for someone who kills a particular fighter — such as granting him the slain man's possessions or some other incentive — this is given to him from the total booty before it is distributed. The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) used to do this on the day of Badr, saying: "Whoever kills a man, that man's possessions are his; and whoever takes a captive, that captive is his." When Allah granted victory over the polytheists — slaying, capturing, and routing them — some of the people asked the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) for what had befallen the enemy. Sa'd ibn 'Ubadah arose among those who had remained to guard the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) and said: "O Messenger of Allah, what prevented us from a share of what these people obtained was neither cowardice before the enemy nor indifference to the reward and the booty — rather, we feared that the enemy might attack you and expose your flank." The people who had gone forward asked that those who remained with the Prophet not receive a share. So Allah Almighty revealed: "They ask you about the spoils — say: the spoils belong to Allah and the Messenger, so fear Allah

<!-- page 211 -->

and set things right among yourselves, and obey Allah and His Messenger if you are believers." [al-Anfal: 1] The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) then commanded that those to whom he had promised a share be given what he had promised them. The commander of the army distributes the booty before the formal oath from his own authority as he sees fit, and then he takes out one-fifth of the booty for the Imam. The remaining four-fifths are divided among those who fought — with themselves and their wealth — such that a horseman receives two shares and a foot soldier receives one share. Whatever is taken without fighting and without approaching the enemy belongs to the Imam. Allah Almighty said: "What Allah has given to His Messenger from the people of the villages belongs to Allah, the Messenger, the near of kin." [al-Hashr: 7] And He said before it: "What Allah restored to His Messenger from them — you spurred neither horse nor camel for it, but Allah gives His messengers power over whom He wills, and Allah is capable of all things." [al-Hashr: 6]

Al-Sadiq Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: If the Imam dispatches a vanguard or an army, or they are part of a larger force, and they advance against the polytheists and fight them, then a fifth of what they take belongs to the Imam and the four-fifths are divided among the fighters — two shares for the horseman and one for the foot soldier — this being when they strive as Allah Almighty has enjoined upon them with their wealth and their persons. If they go out as mercenaries, their share is their wages, as agreed by whoever hired them — whether the Imam or whoever the Imam appointed. Those who attended without fighting receive what the Imam or his deputy grants them as he sees fit. Women who attend receive a share of the booty at the Imam's discretion. Whoever flees from the polytheists' slaves to the Muslims, or is entrusted to them and his master is absent, is free. This is all the position of the Ahl al-Bayt, may Allah's prayers be upon them all.

<!-- page 212 -->

Mentioning the Ruling on Fighting the Rebels:

Allah Almighty said: "And if two groups of believers fight one another, make peace between them. But if one of them transgresses against the other, then fight the one that transgresses until it returns to the command of Allah." [al-Hujurat: 9] Allah Almighty has thus established that fighting the people of rebellion against the Muslims is obligatory — as is the obligation to fight the polytheists — but it is to be carried out under the Imam or whoever the Imam has appointed. It is not for the people to act on their own opinions in this matter: a person may not fight someone he privately judges to be a rebel. Rather, the determination of rebellion belongs to the Imam or to whoever the Imam has appointed. If someone abstains, departs, or goes out against the Imam's authority, the Imam orders the people to fight him, and they are obligated to fight him alongside the Imam. If the Imam ceases fighting for reasons he judges sufficient, they cease fighting alongside him and do not fight except by his permission. The Prophet (peace and blessings be upon him) said: "I was commanded to fight the renegades, the immoral, and the transgressors." The renegades are the people of Basra, the owners of the camel; the immoral are the Kharijites; and the transgressors are the people of the Levant. "I struck them on their backs, bellies, heads, and eyes, and found nothing for them but fighting." To abandon fighting them is akin to abandoning what Allah Almighty revealed to Muhammad (peace be upon him).

It was reported from Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) that he heard Abu Muhammad ibn 'Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) mention those unjust ones whom 'Ali (peace be upon him) fought, and say that they were more sinful than those the Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) fought. He was asked: "O son of the Messenger of Allah, how so?" He said: "Because those were ignorant, while these had read the Qur'an and knew the virtue of those of virtue — yet they turned to what they did after having full insight." The Messenger of Allah (peace and blessings be upon him) said to 'Ali (peace be upon him): "Your war is my war and your peace is my peace" — meaning that whoever fights 'Ali has fought the Messenger of Allah (peace be upon him).


<!-- page 213 -->
He (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) described the Kharijites and said they read the Qur'an yet it does not pass beyond their throats — religion passes through them as an arrow passes through its target. The best of people are those who kill them. He said (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) that 'Ammar was killed by the rebellious group. So Mu'awiyah and his companions killed him, and when they heard the hadith, it weighed heavily upon Mu'awiyah, and the people of Syria were troubled by it. So 'Amr ibn al-'As said to them: It was not we who killed him — rather, Ali and his companions killed him, for it was they who brought him out and were the cause of his death. Word of this reached Ali (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him), and he said: Then by Allah, the Prophet of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) killed those who were martyred with him at Badr.

The Muslims narrated that 'Ammar (may Allah be pleased with him) would never walk along a path between two lines of troops without the people accompanying him, for they had heard from the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) what they had heard. On the day he was killed, while gesturing toward the people of Damascus, he said: "O Allah, You know that I did not fight them except by the command of Your Prophet. I did not know that I would be killed at their hands as I have been told. But if I knew that You are pleased with me, I would kindle a fire and throw myself into it, or, if I could be rid of this beast, I would lower my neck and let them do what they will. O Allah, You know I did not fight them except for Your sake, so do not disappoint my patience and do not give the lie to my hope." He then turned to face the enemy.

'Ammar then looked toward Ali (peace be upon him) and saw him in the company of Hashim ibn 'Utbah. Hashim was a one-eyed man. 'Ammar, seeing him, fixed his gaze on the standard and urged Hashim on, saying: "One-eyed! There is no good in cowardice, and the dress of dishonor does not fade. Carry us forward!" Hashim seized the standard from the ground and said: "A one-eyed man seeks his family. Life must be purchased even at the cost of wealth — or one must flee." He then charged forward, and 'Ammar charged with him, saying: "Charge with Hashim, for death lies on the edges of the spears, and Paradise lies beneath the jars — can you not see the poplar-eyed man with Muhammad and his party in the highest company?" They continued striking, stabbing, and pressing upon the people of Syria

<!-- page 214 -->
until the mercy of Allah descended upon them as martyrs. Two men then disputed with one another over the killing of 'Ammar during the battle, each of them claiming: "I killed him."

His father said: "How remarkable — two men arguing over which of them will enter the Fire. I heard the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) say that the rebellious group killed him." One of them then said that he had killed 'Ammar and taken him into the Fire. Ali (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) mentioned who had killed his companions, and Mu'awiyah said: "He and I will dispute this before the Lord of the Throne on the Day of Resurrection."

Ali said to his companions: If they call out the people of the camel before the fighting begins, that is well, and he prayed for those of the people of the Camel and the people of the River — even those who did not pray and did not know what they were praying for. The banner of guidance had been raised for them, so there is no sin upon those who fought them before the call was made. We have already mentioned the saying of Ali (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) in this regard.

Regarding the killing of the people of rebellion: they are to be fought as polytheists are fought, by every possible means. Those among them who fought — their women and children are also fought. Those who did not fight were not to be killed. Those killed were inherited by their heirs, except for what was brought to the war against the people of justice — that is, what they brought out against them — for if it was captured, it became spoils, to be divided as spoils taken from polytheists are divided. Their descendants and their women are free and are not enslaved. It is permissible to seek the assistance of the families of the rebels against them, just as one may seek the help of Muslims and protected peoples. If they join the people of justice against polytheists in war, they receive shares of four-fifths of the spoils. The Imam of the people of justice has no obligation to the Imam of the rebels. If a rebel requests safe conduct, it shall be granted. As for rebel prisoners, the Imam may execute them if he wishes, or pardon them, or ransom them — but their free persons are not to be enslaved. As for slaves whom they brought with their loyalists, if they are defeated and no longer possess a group to take refuge with, they are left to disperse and are not pursued. Their wounded are not finished off. This is what Ali (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) did when Talha and al-Zubayr were killed and 'A'ishah was surrounded — since they had no remaining group to rally — when he ordered his companions not to pursue the fleeing, nor to kill those who cast down their weapons, nor to finish off the wounded.

<!-- page 215 -->
If, however, they still have a group to rally to, then when they are routed, they are to be pursued and struck down. Ali (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) also commanded this regarding the people of Syria on the day of Siffin: when Mu'awiyah's forces were routed, he ordered his companions to pursue them and cut them down — until they raised the Qur'ans, and his companions found nothing to do but comply with what their own command demanded. They thus turned away from their pursuit when the rebels called out for arbitration by the Qur'an — the scheme and stratagem by which 'Amr ibn al-'As deceived Mu'awiyah.

When the people of justice prevail over the people of rebellion, they are to restore what had been seized, deducted, appropriated, or taken by betrayal, returning it to its rightful owners. Merchants among the rebels, whether their wives or their offspring, if they were with the rebel forces, their goods are booty. If they were in a city and not engaged in the war against the people of justice, nor providing assistance, nor bringing supplies, then their goods are not booty for the people of justice even if the people of justice prevailed. The ruling on dividing such booty follows the ruling applied in the case of polytheists.

Whoever is killed by the people of justice from among the rebels — in their wars, in their homes, or in their battles — so long as they remain rebels, there is no liability upon the people of justice for what they have suffered or for the spoils taken from them. If the rebels turn away and return to the truth and submit to the judgment of justice, then all that was done to them in the course of fighting was rightly done, by Allah's will and His empowering them and commanding them to do so.

As for wealth taken by the rebels during their rebellion from the people of justice, they are obliged to return it to its owners. If it is established and known who took it, it shall be taken from them, since they took it without right. If it is not known who took it, there remains nothing but an oath against those who claim it. Whoever among the people of justice was killed and his killer is known, the relatives of the slain may exact retaliation from that killer, since he killed him unjustly. If they do so, that is the reality of repentance and redress of wrongs. As for what befalls the rebels among themselves,

<!-- page 216 -->
the ruling of the people of justice does not apply between them, and it is not considered unless the one in authority over them comes upon it through his own proper course — then he shall adjudicate it if it is established. If the people who are sound among the rebels request a truce, the matter is put before the people of justice, and their leader does what he deems best for himself and for the Muslims among his companions, until he is able to fight them. If he grants them a pledge, he shall honor it. If he wishes to fight them afterward, he must first renounce their covenant, as we have mentioned in the case of polytheists. He must not reconcile with them in any matter that involves abandoning the limits of Allah or forbidding what He has permitted or leaving any of His rights. Nor should this prevent a man of religion from killing his father who is among the rebels, unless he fears him or cannot find anyone to do it for him. If he finds others to act in his stead without him, he should leave it to them. This is the collective saying of the Ahl al-Bayt (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon them).

**On those among the People of the Qibla who must be fought and killed:**

Whoever denies one of the obligations of Islam or denies any of its dues is a denier of his duty. He is to be fought if he resorts to war and refuses to return to the truth, along with those who stand behind him or assist him — for they are fought and killed until the command of Allah is fulfilled. As for brigands who set upon a people intending to seize their wealth, it is permissible for those people to fight them and kill them without any prior condition, and it is not obligatory upon those with them from among the Muslims and the Covenanted to fight them, though they are also obligated to wage jihad.

Abu Ja'far Muhammad ibn Ali (may Allah's prayers be upon him) was asked about a man who is killed defending his property. He said: The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: "Whoever is killed defending his wealth is a martyr" — even if he left the money and did not fight for it when he feared that defending it would lead to his own destruction, for abandoning wealth is easier than courting destruction. Whoever does that has the reward

<!-- page 217 -->
of one who stands firm in the right — because the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) declared the one killed in that cause a martyr.

Ja'far ibn Muhammad (may Allah's prayers be upon him) said: A man came to Ali (peace be upon him) and said: "O Commander of the Faithful, a man came to my wife and stole her jewelry while I was watching, and I was too afraid to confront him." Ali (peace be upon him) said: "But had it been Ali ibn Abi Talib, he would not have accepted that even if they raised the sword against him." If someone is not harmed at the hands of thieves, and his family knows the thief, the stolen goods are to be returned to them. If the family does not know who the thief is, it falls upon those who fought him. The Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) said: The spoils of brigands are lawful, and the spy and informant may be killed by any means available. If the Imam sees fit to imprison or punish the brigand as he deems appropriate, or to release him, he may do so until the brigand repents in prison.

As for the apostate — his path is the path of the captive, even if he is one of the polytheists, and no covenant or safe conduct is accepted from him. If the Imam wishes to enslave him, he may. Whoever was born into Islam and then becomes a Jew, a Christian, or otherwise differs from Islam is to be killed and is not to be given time to repent unless he repents before he is apprehended, in accordance with the words of the Messenger of Allah (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him): "Whoever changes his religion, kill him" — meaning those who convert away from Islam. If, however, a Jew becomes Christian, or a Christian becomes Jewish, or one passes from one form of disbelief to another, he is not to be interfered with, for all of that is disbelief — one thing and one word. Such a person is not called an apostate. The apostate is one who leaves a religion for its opposite. If a polytheist submits to Islam and then returns to polytheism, the matter is referred to the sultan, and he is given three days to repent. If he does not repent by the third day, he is killed. This is what Ali (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) did with an apostate who was brought to him. If apostates collectively refuse, they are fought as polytheists are fought. An apostate woman is imprisoned until she repents in prison. The sleeping sect is beaten but not fed except what is needed for her imprisonment, and no tax is taken from her except what is hidden from her followers — and they are commanded to deal with her more harshly until she repents or dies. She should not be allowed to benefit her allies through imprisonment.

If apostates, together with their wives, refuse to convert to Islam and entrench themselves in a fortress or district, they are fought as polytheists are fought, and if they are vanquished and do not repent,

<!-- page 218 -->
their wives and offspring are enslaved. Women are pressed to convert to Islam as apostate slave-women are pressed. They are not to settle down or be given in marriage, for it is not permissible to marry an apostate. When their children reach the age of discernment, Islam is to be presented to them, and their path is the path of apostate women and men.

The heretics are killed even after they repent — they are not given time to repent — and they are burned with fire after being killed. This is what Ali (may Allah's peace and blessings be upon him) did with a group of people who came to him and exaggerated in glorifying him, claiming that he was God, Glorified and Exalted be He above what they claimed. He called them to repent, and they refused and insisted, saying: "If you kill us, you will revive us." He was filled with anger. He ordered a pit to be dug, and fire was kindled in it. He said: "Today I will fill you with flesh and fat." He then commanded his servant Qanbar to strike their necks, and he threw them into that pit and burned them in it. He said, upon seeing what he saw that day:

*I kindled a fire and called upon a fire.*

---

Here ends the first part of the book *Summary of Archaeology*. May Allah bless our master Muhammad al-Mukhtar and his righteous family. This copy was completed on the 21st of Jumada al-Ula, 1437 AH. We ask you to pray for the scribe of this book.
